<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Every sequence of generalized log canonical flips on a normal irreducible globally Weil ℚ-factorial compact Kähler fourfold terminates, provided the flips are projective small diagrams with the stated opposite ample signs. The boundary is rational, and the nef b-divisor is fixed and represented by an analytically nef ℚ-Cartier divisor on a projective modification. No scaling rule or pseudo-effectivity assumption is required. The theorem concerns existing flip sequences; it does not assert the existence of all contractions or flips.

- **claim-glc-flip-termination** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $X_0$ be a normal irreducible globally Weil $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial compact Kähler fourfold. Let $B_0$ be an effective rational Weil divisor of finite support with coefficients in $[0,1]$. Let $\mathbf{M}$ be represented by an analytically nef $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor on a projective bimeromorphic normal compact Kähler model of $X_0$. Fix compatible actual canonical divisor representatives on all birational models under consideration. Suppose that $D_0=K_{X_0}+B_0+M_{X_0}$ is $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier and that $(X_0,B_0+\mathbf{M})$ is generalized log canonical.* *Consider a sequence of normal irreducible globally Weil $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial compact Kähler fourfolds $$X_0\dashrightarrow X_1\dashrightarrow X_2\dashrightarrow\cdots.$$ On $X_i$, let $B_i$ be the strict transform of $B_0$, let $M_i=M_{X_i}$ be the trace of the same b-divisor, and assume that $D_i=K_{X_i}+B_i+M_i$ is $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier. Transport the canonical representatives under the induced identifications of prime divisors. Suppose that every step is given by a diagram $$\label{eq:flip-diagram} \begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large] X_i \arrow[r,"f_i"] & Z_i & X_{i+1}\arrow[l,"f_i^+"'] \end{tikzcd}$$ where $Z_i$ is normal compact Kähler, both maps are projective small bimeromorphic morphisms with connected fibers, neither is an isomorphism, and $$-D_i\text{ is }f_i\text{-ample},\qquad D_{i+1}\text{ is }f_i^+\text{-ample}.$$ Here *small* means that the exceptional locus contains no prime divisor. Then the sequence is finite.
  - Explanation: Every prescribed flip sequence satisfying the stated global factoriality, generalized log canonical, rationality, projectivity and ample-sign conditions is finite. The theorem permits varying bases and arbitrary choices of qualifying diagrams.
- **claim-finite-low-places** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-low-places)): Let $(T,B+\mathbf{M})$ be gklt rational generalized data on a normal compact Kähler space, with effective boundary of finite support and an absolutely nef rational datum carried by a projective modification. Fix a projective log resolution carrying the datum. There is $\varepsilon>0$ such that every global place satisfies $$a(E;T,B+\mathbf{M})\geq\varepsilon,$$ and only finitely many places exceptional over $T$ satisfy $$a(E;T,B+\mathbf{M})<1+\varepsilon.$$ This finite set can be represented simultaneously by prime divisors on a smooth model projective over $T$. In particular the exceptional places of discrepancy at most one form a finite set with such a realization.
  - Explanation: A fixed rational generalized klt pair has a positive discrepancy lower bound and finitely many exceptional places below the indicated threshold. Those places admit simultaneous projective realization.
- **claim-exact-extraction** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-extraction)): Let $(T,B+\mathbf{M})$ be rational gklt data on a strong normal compact Kähler space, with an absolutely nef rational line bundle on a projective carrier and rationally invertible adjoint. For any specified finite set $\mathcal E$ of places exceptional over $T$ with $a(E;T,B+\mathbf{M})\leq1$, there is a projective bimeromorphic morphism $h:U\to T$ such that $U$ is a strong normal compact Kähler space, the $h$-exceptional prime divisors are exactly the members of $\mathcal E$, and the crepant generalized pair on $U$ is gklt with effective boundary. Thus, writing this boundary as $B_U$, $$K_U+B_U+M_U=h^*(K_T+B+M_T).$$
  - Explanation: A specified finite collection of low-discrepancy exceptional places can be extracted crepantly on a strong model with effective boundary. Generalized terminality requires extracting all exceptional places of discrepancy at most one.
- **claim-relative-continuation-and-nef-run** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-relative-program)): Let $\pi:T\to V$ be a projective bimeromorphic morphism of normal compact Kähler spaces. Suppose that $T$ is strong and that $(T,B+\mathbf{M})$ is rational gdlt, with effective boundary, a projective carrier on which the rational line datum is nef over $V$, and a rationally invertible adjoint $D_T$.* 1. *If $D_T$ is not relatively nef over $V$, there is an elementary negative step over $V$. Its output is projective over $V$, normal compact Kähler and strong, and is gdlt with the transported boundary and b-data. The same construction is available after every finite prefix whose last adjoint is not relatively nef over $V$.* 2. *If the initial pair is gklt, there exists a finite, possibly empty, sequence of elementary negative steps over this one fixed base $V$ whose last adjoint is relatively nef over $V$. Its models and transported data have the properties in the first assertion.
  - Explanation: Generalized dlt data admit continuation whenever the relative adjoint is not nef. Generalized klt data admit a finite run to a nef adjoint over the fixed bimeromorphic base; this assertion alone does not establish termination of every possible run.
- **claim-crepant-gdlt-modification** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-gdlt-modification)): Let $(T,B+\mathbf{M})$ be rational glc data of dimension at most four on a normal compact Kähler space. Assume that the higher absolutely nef rational line datum is carried by a projective modification and that $K_T+B+M_T$ is rationally invertible. There is a projective bimeromorphic morphism $h:Y\to T$ such that $Y$ is a strong normal compact Kähler space, $(Y,B_Y+\mathbf{M})$ is gdlt with $B_Y\geq0$, and $$K_Y+B_Y+M_Y=h^*(K_T+B+M_T).$$ Every $h$-exceptional prime has coefficient one in $B_Y$, equivalently has generalized log discrepancy zero over $(T,B+\mathbf{M})$.
  - Explanation: Rational generalized log canonical data admit a projective crepant generalized dlt modification on a strong model. Each exceptional prime of the modification has coefficient one, without requiring extraction of every zero-discrepancy place.
- **claim-special-termination** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-special-termination)): Let $d\leq4$, and let $$(T_0,B_0+\mathbf{M})\dashrightarrow(T_1,B_1+\mathbf{M}) \dashrightarrow(T_2,B_2+\mathbf{M})\dashrightarrow\cdots$$ be a sequence of elementary steps for rational gdlt data on strong normal compact Kähler spaces of dimension $d$. The boundaries are transported as in an elementary program, and the higher absolutely nef b-line datum is fixed and is carried by projective modifications. There is an index $i_0$ such that, for every $i\geq i_0$, the exceptional loci on both sides of the step are disjoint from every generalized log canonical center on their respective sides. The contraction bases in this sequence may vary.
  - Explanation: An elementary generalized dlt sequence eventually avoids every generalized log canonical center on both sides of each step, even when the contraction bases vary.
- **claim-finite-common-projective-model** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-common-projective-models)): Let a finite collection of normal irreducible compact Kähler spaces be joined, with compatible bimeromorphic identifications, by finitely many projective bimeromorphic morphisms and their inverses. Suppose that a fixed nef b-divisor is carried by a projective bimeromorphic morphism $X'\to X$ to one of these spaces, with analytically nef $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier trace $M'$ on $X'$. Then there is a smooth compact Kähler space $W$ with compatible projective bimeromorphic morphisms to every space in the collection and to $X'$. The resolution may simultaneously principalize finitely many specified generically nonzero coherent ideals and resolve finitely many specified divisors. Its trace $M_W$ is the pullback of $M'$ and is analytically nef.
  - Explanation: A finite compatible birational configuration and its nef carrier admit a common smooth compact Kähler model with projective maps. Simultaneous resolution and principalization preserve the pulled-back analytic nef datum.
- **claim-effective-nef-defect** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-nef-defect)): Let $T$ be a normal compact Kähler model, and let $r:W\to T$ be a projective bimeromorphic morphism from a normal compact Kähler space $W$ dominating the fixed projective carrier of $\mathbf{M}$. Assume that $M_T$ is $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier. Then $$\label{eq:nef-defect} J_{W/T}:=r^*M_T-M_W\geq0$$ is an exceptional $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor over $T$. If $s:W'\to W$ is a higher normal model, then $J_{W'/T}=s^*J_{W/T}$.
  - Explanation: The pullback of the target nef trace differs from the descending higher trace by an effective exceptional divisor. The defect pulls back compatibly on higher models.
- **claim-global-to-local-singularities** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-ordinary-singularities)): Let $T$ be a normal compact Kähler space, let $B$ be an effective rational divisor of finite support, and let $\mathbf{M}$ have analytically nef rational data on a projective carrier. Assume that $K_T$, $B$, and $M_T$ are $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier. If every global generalized log discrepancy of $(T,B+\mathbf{M})$ is nonnegative, respectively positive, then the same condition holds for local analytic places. In the positive case, $T$ has ordinary klt singularities locally. If the global generalized discrepancies are positive and every global place exceptional over $T$ has generalized log discrepancy strictly greater than one, then $T$ has ordinary terminal singularities locally. In dimension four the latter conclusion implies $\dim\mathop{\mathrm{Sing}}T\leq1$.
  - Explanation: Global generalized discrepancy inequalities imply their local analytic counterparts. Effective boundary and effective nef defect also yield ordinary klt or terminal singularities under the corresponding strict inequalities.
- **claim-small-diagram-discrepancy-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-small-comparison)): Let $$T\xrightarrow{\ f\ }Z\xleftarrow{\ f^+\ }T^+$$ be a diagram of normal irreducible compact Kähler fourfolds in which $f$ and $f^+$ are projective small bimeromorphic morphisms with connected fibers, and both are nonisomorphisms. Let $(T,B_T+\mathbf{M})$ and $(T^+,B_{T^+}+\mathbf{M})$ have effective rational boundaries related by strict transform and the same fixed rational b-divisor. Transport the canonical representatives by the identification of prime divisors, and assume that the actual adjoints $D_T$ and $D_{T^+}$ are $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier, with $-D_T$ $f$-ample and $D_{T^+}$ $f^+$-ample.* *The exceptional images of $f$ and $f^+$ are equal. If their common image is $A$, then $$\label{eq:small-loci} L:=\mathop{\mathrm{Exc}}(f)=f^{-1}(A),\qquad L^+:=\mathop{\mathrm{Exc}}(f^+)=(f^+)^{-1}(A),$$ the complements are isomorphic, and $$\label{eq:small-dimensions} \dim L,\dim L^+\leq2,\qquad \dim A\leq1, \qquad \dim L+\dim L^+\geq3.$$* *Let $G$ be the normalization of the main graph, with projections $p:G\to T$, $q:G\to T^+$ and $h=f\circ p=f^+\circ q$. Then the actual $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor $$\label{eq:small-divisor} F:=p^*D_T-q^*D_{T^+}$$ is effective and exceptional over both $T$ and $T^+$, and $$\label{eq:small-support} \mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}F=h^{-1}(A).$$ Moreover, $F\cdot C<0$ for every irreducible curve $C$ contracted by $h$.* *For every global place $E$ over these spaces, let $\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E(F)$ denote the coefficient of the pullback of $F$ on any higher proper model dominating $G$ and carrying $E$. Then $$\label{eq:small-discrepancies} a(E;T^+,B_{T^+}+\mathbf{M})-a(E;T,B_T+\mathbf{M})=\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E(F)\geq0.$$ The inequality is strict exactly when $\mathop{\mathrm{cent}}_T(E)\subset L$, and this condition is equivalent to $\mathop{\mathrm{cent}}_{T^+}(E)\subset L^+$. A place is exceptional over $T$ if and only if it is exceptional over $T^+$.
  - Explanation: The two small morphisms have the same exceptional image. Their graph carries an effective adjoint correction supported exactly over that image, so discrepancies increase precisely for places centered in the exceptional loci and exceptionality is preserved.
- **claim-finite-program-effective-correction** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-program-comparison)): Let $Y_0\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow Y_m$, with $m\geq0$, be a finite sequence of normal irreducible compact Kähler fourfolds with $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier adjoints $D_j=K_{Y_j}+B_j+M_{Y_j}$. Suppose the effective rational boundaries are transported by pushforward, the canonical representatives are compatible, and the same b-divisor with a projective carrier is used throughout. Assume each step is either a small diagram satisfying Lemma <a href="#lem:small-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:small-comparison">11</a>, or a projective bimeromorphic divisorial contraction $v_j:Y_j\to Y_{j+1}$ such that $D_{j+1}=(v_j)_*D_j$ and $-D_j$ is $v_j$-ample. These steps extract no prime divisors. On a smooth common model $W$ with projective maps to every $Y_j$ and to the carrier, write $r_j:W\to Y_j$. Then $$\label{eq:program-comparison} r_0^*D_0-r_m^*D_m=E,\qquad E\geq0,\qquad (r_m)_*E=0.$$ Every global discrepancy is nondecreasing along the run. If a prime divisor is contracted in a divisorial step, its discrepancy increases strictly at that step, and its discrepancy at the end is strictly larger than before that step.
  - Explanation: A finite negative program accumulates an effective correction exceptional over its endpoint. Discrepancies never decrease, and the strict increase caused by contracting a prime cannot be canceled later.
- **claim-nef-end-factors-through-ample-model** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-nef-ample)): Let $Y_0$, $Y$, and $T_+$ be normal irreducible compact Kähler spaces with projective bimeromorphic morphisms to a normal compact Kähler space $S$. On a smooth common model $W$ over $S$, projective over these spaces, let $P_0$, $P$, and $P_+$ be the pullbacks of respective $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisors $D_0$, $D_Y$, and $D_+$. Suppose that $$P_0=P+E_Y=P_++E_+,$$ where $E_Y\geq0$ is exceptional over $Y$ and $E_+\geq0$ is exceptional over $T_+$. If $D_Y$ is nef over $S$ and $D_+$ is ample over $S$, then $P=P_+$ as actual divisors. The induced bimeromorphic map is a projective morphism $\varphi:Y\to T_+$ over $S$, and $D_Y=\varphi^*D_+$.
  - Explanation: Effective comparisons with the same initial pullback force the nef endpoint and prescribed ample model to have equal adjoint pullbacks. The endpoint maps projectively to the ample model and its adjoint is the pullback of the ample adjoint.
- **claim-analytic-cycle-rank-loss** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cycle-loss)): Let $k\geq0$ be an integer, and let $X\dashrightarrow Y$ be a bimeromorphic transformation of compact Kähler spaces represented by a proper bimeromorphic diagram. Suppose it identifies $$U=X\setminus A\simeq Y\setminus B,$$ where $A$ and $B$ are closed analytic subspaces and $\dim B<k$. Then restriction to $U$ and strict transform induce a surjection $\mathcal C_k(X)\to\mathcal C_k(Y)$. Consequently $c_k(X)\geq c_k(Y)$, with strict inequality if $A$ contains an irreducible compact analytic subspace of dimension $k$.* *In a sequence of bimeromorphic transformations of normal irreducible $n$-dimensional compact Kähler spaces which extract no prime divisors, only finitely many transformations can contract a prime divisor; a small transformation preserves $c_{n-1}$. In particular, for a small fourfold diagram of Lemma <a href="#lem:small-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:small-comparison">11</a>, if $\dim L^+<2$, then $c_2(T)\geq c_2(T^+)$, strictly if $L$ contains a surface.
  - Explanation: Strict transform induces a surjection on analytic cycle spans when the target exceptional complement is sufficiently small. A source cycle in the removed locus gives a strict rank drop, and this controls divisorial contractions and negative-side exceptional surfaces.
- **claim-crepant-terminal-junctions** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-terminal-junctions)): Suppose that the sequence above is infinite. After passing to a tail, there are a number $\varepsilon>0$ and a finite set $\mathcal I$ of global places such that, at every remaining index $i$, $$\label{eq:stable-low-places} \begin{gathered} a_i(E)\geq\varepsilon\quad\text{for every global place }E, \\ \mathcal I= \{E:E\text{ is exceptional over }X_i,\ a_i(E)\leq 1\}. \end{gathered}$$ There are projective bimeromorphic crepant morphisms $$\label{eq:terminal-junction} \begin{gathered} h_i:(Y_i,\Theta_i+\mathbf{M})\longrightarrow (X_i,B_i+\mathbf{M}), \\ K_{Y_i}+\Theta_i+M_{Y_i}=h_i^*D_i, \end{gathered}$$ with the following properties.* 1. *Each $Y_i$ is a strong normal irreducible compact Kähler fourfold, $\Theta_i$ is effective, and $(Y_i,\Theta_i+\mathbf{M})$ is generalized terminal. The exceptional prime divisors of $h_i$ represent exactly the places in $\mathcal I$.* 2. *For $E\in\mathcal I$, let $S_{i,E}$ be its prime on $Y_i$. Define $$\label{eq:junction-coefficient-change} \Theta_i'= \Theta_i-\sum_{E\in\mathcal I} \bigl(a_{i+1}(E)-a_i(E)\bigr)S_{i,E}.$$ Starting with $(Y_i,\Theta_i'+\mathbf{M})$, a finite, possibly empty, sequence of small elementary steps over $Z_i$ ends at $(Y_{i+1},\Theta_{i+1}+\mathbf{M})$. All its models are strong normal irreducible compact Kähler fourfolds, all its pairs are generalized terminal, and its boundary and nef data are transported as in an elementary program. In particular, every global place is exceptional over one model of this connecting sequence if and only if it is exceptional over all of them.* 3. *There is one finite label set $\mathcal H$ for the primes supplied by $\mathcal I$ and by the components of $B_0$. Write $S_h$ for the current strict transform of a label and $b_h$ for its current coefficient. Labels are retained when their coefficient is zero. On the entire chain of coefficient changes and small steps, $$\Theta=\sum_{h\in\mathcal H}b_hS_h, \qquad 0\leq b_h<1,$$ and every $b_h$ is nonincreasing. Call a label *varying* if its coefficient sequence is not eventually constant, and *fixed* otherwise. After a further tail is chosen, all fixed coefficients are constant. Every varying label comes from $\mathcal I$ and has positive coefficient at every remaining stage.
  - Explanation: Under the hypothetical infinite strong generalized klt sequence, a tail has a stable finite set of low places. Extracting them gives generalized terminal junctions connected by coefficient decreases and finite, possibly empty, small programs with persistent labels.
- **claim-marked-surface-discrepancy-finiteness** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-surface-finiteness)): In the setting of Proposition <a href="#prop:terminal-junctions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:terminal-junctions">15</a>, there is a finite set $$\mathcal A_{\mathrm{surf}} \subset [\varepsilon,1]\cap\mathbb{Q}$$ such that $a_i(E)\in\mathcal A_{\mathrm{surf}}$ whenever $E\in\mathcal I$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{cent}}_{X_i}(E)$ is a surface. After passing to a further tail, the following conclusions hold.* 1. *No $E\in\mathcal I$ has a surface center contained in $L_i$ on $X_i$, or a surface center contained in $L_i^+$ on $X_{i+1}$.* 2. *Every varying label $S_h\subset Y_i$ satisfies $\dim h_i(S_h)\leq1$ at every junction.* 3. *For an irreducible compact analytic surface contained in either $L_i$ or $L_i^+$, the corresponding junction morphism is an isomorphism near its general point, and the downstairs model is smooth there. Blowing up the coherent ideal of that surface defines a unique global generic blowup place. It is exceptional over the corresponding downstairs model and has discrepancy strictly greater than $1$; in particular it does not belong to $\mathcal I$.
  - Explanation: Marked surface discrepancies lie in a fixed finite rational set. On a further tail, varying labels have only curve or point images, and exceptional surfaces have smooth general points with unmarked global generic blowup places.
- **claim-branch-excess-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-branch)): Let $p\colon Y\to X$ be a projective bimeromorphic morphism of normal irreducible compact Kähler fourfolds, where $Y$ has ordinary terminal singularities. Let $Q=\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}Q_j$ be a reduced union of distinct irreducible divisors satisfying $\dim p(Q)\leq1$. Then the sum defining $\beta(Q)$ and the count defining $\tau_Y(Q)$ are finite, and $$\label{eq:branch-inequality} \beta(Q)\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}c_2(Q_j^\nu)+\tau_Y(Q).$$ The rank $c_2(Q_j^\nu)$ is the rational rank spanned in $H_4(Q_j^\nu,\mathbb{Q})$ by the fundamental classes of compact irreducible analytic surfaces.
  - Explanation: For divisors contracted to curves or points from an ordinary terminal fourfold, branch excess is finite and bounded by normalization surface-cycle ranks and the count of global ordinary discrepancy-two places over singular curves. The cycle ranks are homological ranks, not Chern classes.
- **claim-normalization-weight-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-branch-normalization)): For the spaces in <a href="#eq:branch-projective-loci" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:branch-projective-loci">[eq:branch-projective-loci]</a>, the sum defining $\beta(Q)$ is finite and $$\label{eq:branch-normalization-bound} \beta(Q)-\sum_jc_2(Q_j^\nu) \leq \mathop{\mathrm{rk}}\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}^W_4\bigl[H^5(P)\longrightarrow H^5(Q)\bigr].$$
  - Explanation: Normalization excision bounds branch excess after subtracting normalization cycle ranks by the weight-four rank of the restriction map in degree-five cohomology.
- **claim-analytic-perverse-decomposition** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-branch-analytic-decomposition)): For $f=g$ with target $Y$, and for $f=pg$ with target $X$, the rational complex $Rf_*\mathbb{Q}_{\widetilde Y}[4]$ is a finite direct sum of shifts of perverse middle extensions with irreducible closed analytic supports. Its unique full-support summand is the intersection complex of the target in perverse degree zero.
  - Explanation: The resolution and its composite with the contraction admit finite decompositions into shifted perverse middle extensions with analytic supports. The intersection complex is the unique full-support summand.
- **claim-resolved-inverse-image-purity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-branch-resolved-purity)): The restriction map $$H^5(\widetilde Y)\longrightarrow H^5(\widetilde P)$$ is surjective. The mixed Hodge structure on the projective variety $\widetilde P$ is pure of weight $5$ in this degree.
  - Explanation: Restriction from the compact Kähler resolution surjects onto the cohomology of the resolved inverse image. Hodge-filtration compatibility and strictness then eliminate the lower-weight part in the stated degree.
- **claim-singular-curve-cohomology-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-branch-cone)): With the choices in <a href="#eq:branch-local-systems" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:branch-local-systems">[eq:branch-local-systems]</a>, $$\label{eq:branch-cone-bound} \mathop{\mathrm{rk}}\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}^W_4\bigl[H^5(P)\longrightarrow H^5(Q)\bigr] \leq \sum_{\substack{T\text{ irreducible curve}\\T\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Sing}}Y\cap Q}} b_T.$$
  - Explanation: The relevant weight-graded rank is bounded by the compactly supported local-system contributions of singular curves contained in the divisor union.
- **claim-resolution-to-punctured-local-system-quotient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-link-quotient)): For every singular curve $T$ with the choices in <a href="#eq:branch-local-systems" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:branch-local-systems">[eq:branch-local-systems]</a>, restriction from the resolution to the regular locus induces a surjection of local systems $$\label{eq:branch-link-quotient} \mathcal W_T\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal V_T.$$ For every $t\in T^\circ$, its map on fibers is equivariant for the action of $\pi_1(T^\circ,t)$ and is the map induced by restriction $$\label{eq:branch-link-stalk-map} \begin{aligned} (\mathcal W_T)_t &\simeq H^2(g^{-1}(t)) \simeq\varinjlim_{U\ni t}H^2(g^{-1}U)\\ &\longrightarrow\varinjlim_{U\ni t}H^2(U\cap Y_{\mathrm{reg}}) \simeq(\mathcal V_T)_t, \end{aligned}$$ where $U$ runs through sufficiently small open neighborhoods of $t$ in $Y$ and $g^{-1}(U\cap Y_{\mathrm{reg}})$ is identified with $U\cap Y_{\mathrm{reg}}$.
  - Explanation: Resolution-fiber cohomology surjects naturally onto punctured-neighborhood cohomology along each singular curve. The stalk restriction maps respect monodromy.
- **claim-global-discrepancy-two-curve-places** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-curve-places)): For every irreducible curve component $T$ of $\mathop{\mathrm{Sing}}Y$, with $b_T$ as in <a href="#eq:branch-local-systems" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:branch-local-systems">[eq:branch-local-systems]</a> and the fixed resolution $g:\widetilde Y\to Y$, $$b_T\ \le\ \#\left\{ \begin{array}{c} E\text{ a prime component of }\mathop{\mathrm{Exc}}(g):\\ g(E)=T,\quad a(E;Y,0)=2 \end{array} \right\}.$$ In particular, the divisors counted here represent distinct global divisorial places.
  - Explanation: Each singular-curve contribution is bounded by distinct global discrepancy-two exceptional divisors on the fixed resolution. Retaining the curve monodromy distinguishes global divisors from separate local branches.
- **claim-finite-corrected-low-discrepancy-support** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-difficulty-finite)): Let $(Y,\Theta+\mathbf{M})$ be a model of the truncated terminal chain. Among the places exceptional over $Y$ with discrepancy less than two, there are only finitely many whose centers have codimension at least three. For each fixed irreducible compact analytic surface $V\subset Y$, there are only finitely many whose center is $V$.* *There is a finite set $\mathcal V_Y$ of irreducible compact analytic surfaces with the following additional property. If $V\notin\mathcal V_Y$, its general point lies in a smooth open where $\mathbf{M}$ descends and either no label occurs or exactly one smooth label $S_h$ occurs. There are no positive-weight places centered on $V$ in the first case or when $b_h=0$. When $b_h>0$, they are exactly the echoes $E_{V,h,j}$ with $j(1-b_h)<1$.
  - Explanation: Positive-weight exceptional places are finite above each fixed surface and at centers of higher codimension. Away from finitely many exceptional surfaces, their only recurring contributions are the explicitly described echoes of a sole positive label.
- **claim-junction-difficulty-nonnegative** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-difficulty-lower)): At every crepant junction $h_i:(Y_i,\Theta_i+\mathbf{M})\to(X_i,B_i+\mathbf{M})$ of the truncated chain, $$\mathfrak D(Y_i,\Theta_i+\mathbf{M})\geq0.$$
  - Explanation: The branch inequality compensates for the possible rounding deficit in the local surface brackets. Consequently the difficulty is nonnegative at crepant junctions.
- **claim-normalization-cycle-rank-cancellation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-difficulty-ranks)): Consider a small step of the terminal chain, with exceptional loci $L,L^+$, $$Y\xrightarrow{f}Z\xleftarrow{f^+}Y^+.$$ For each label $h$, let $e_h$ and $e_h^+$ be the numbers of prime divisors in $S_h^\nu$ and $(S_h^+)^\nu$ lying over $L$ and $L^+$, respectively. Then these numbers are finite and $$\label{eq:difficulty-rank-change} \begin{gathered} e_h=\sum_{\substack{V\subset L\\\dim V=2}}r_{V,h},\qquad e_h^+=\sum_{\substack{V^+\subset L^+\\\dim V^+=2}}r_{V^+,h}^+,\\ c_2(S_h^\nu)-c_2((S_h^+)^\nu)=e_h-e_h^+. \end{gathered}$$ The surface sums run over irreducible compact analytic surfaces.
  - Explanation: The change in each label normalization's surface-cycle rank equals the difference in its contracted prime counts. This identity cancels the change in surface defaults in the difficulty comparison.
- **claim-difficulty-monotonicity** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-difficulty-change)): The difficulty is nonincreasing under every coefficient change and every small step of the truncated terminal chain. More precisely:* 1. *Suppose $\Theta'\leq\Theta$ is a coefficient change on the same model $Y$, and write $w(E)$ and $w'(E)$ for its old and new weights. The set $$\mathcal C=\{E:\ E\text{ exceptional over }Y,\ w(E)\ne w'(E)\}$$ is finite, and $$\label{eq:difficulty-coefficient-change} \mathfrak D(Y,\Theta+\mathbf{M})-\mathfrak D(Y,\Theta'+\mathbf{M}) =\sum_{E\in\mathcal C}\bigl(w(E)-w'(E)\bigr)\geq0.$$* 2. *For a small step $Y\to Z\leftarrow Y^+$ with exceptional loci $L,L^+$, write $w(E),w^+(E)$ for the two weights. The set $$\mathcal P=\{E:\ E\text{ exceptional over }Y,\ \mathop{\mathrm{cent}}_Y(E)\subset L,\ w(E)+w^+(E)>0\}$$ is finite. Its center condition is equivalently $\mathop{\mathrm{cent}}_{Y^+}(E)\subset L^+$, and $$\label{eq:difficulty-small-change} \mathfrak D(Y,\Theta+\mathbf{M})-\mathfrak D(Y^+,\Theta^++\mathbf{M}) =\sum_{E\in\mathcal P}\bigl(w(E)-w^+(E)\bigr)\geq0.$$* *In either move, a strict decrease in the weight of any exceptional place forces a strict decrease in the difficulty.
  - Explanation: Coefficient decreases and small steps change difficulty by finite sums of nonnegative exceptional weight losses. Any strict exceptional weight loss causes a strict difficulty decrease.
- **claim-positive-surface-difficulty-drop** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-positive-surface)): Use the truncated terminal chain of Propositions <a href="#prop:terminal-junctions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:terminal-junctions">15</a> and <a href="#prop:surface-finiteness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:surface-finiteness">16</a>, with the integer $N$, weights, and constant defaults chosen in Section <a href="#sec:difficulty" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:difficulty">6</a>. If an irreducible compact analytic surface $V$ is contained in $L_i^+\subset X_{i+1}$, there is a global place $E$, exceptional over both junctions and every model of their connector, such that $$a_{i+1}(E)\in(1,2]\cap N^{-1}\mathbb{Z}, \qquad w_i(E)\geq w_{i+1}(E)+1.$$ Here $w_i$ and $w_{i+1}$ are the weights at the two crepant junctions. Consequently $$\mathfrak D(Y_i,\Theta_i+\mathbf{M}) >\mathfrak D(Y_{i+1},\Theta_{i+1}+\mathbf{M}).$$
  - Explanation: A positive-side exceptional surface supplies a global generic blowup place whose endpoint discrepancy lies in the fixed rational lattice. Strict discrepancy improvement therefore forces a strict ceiling-weight loss and a strict junction difficulty drop.
- **claim-strong-gklt-termination** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-strong-gklt)): Every sequence satisfying the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> for which all the models $X_i$ are globally strongly $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial and the initial pair $(X_0,B_0+\mathbf{M})$ is gklt is finite. The small diagrams may be chosen arbitrarily subject to the hypotheses of that theorem.
  - Explanation: On strong generalized klt models, difficulty descent allows only finitely many positive exceptional surfaces. Every remaining step strictly lowers the ambient surface-cycle rank, ruling out an infinite tail.
- **claim-elementary-gdlt-termination** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-gdlt)): Let $$(T_0,B_0+\mathbf{M})\dashrightarrow(T_1,B_1+\mathbf{M}) \dashrightarrow(T_2,B_2+\mathbf{M})\dashrightarrow\cdots$$ be a sequence of elementary steps for rational gdlt pairs on strong normal irreducible compact Kähler fourfolds. Assume that the boundaries are effective rational divisors of finite support transported by the steps, that the fixed b-divisor is represented by an analytically nef $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor on a projective bimeromorphic normal compact Kähler model of $T_0$, and that the actual adjoints are $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier with compatible canonical representatives. Then the sequence is finite. The contraction bases and the choices of elementary steps may vary.
  - Explanation: Every qualifying elementary generalized dlt program terminates, with varying bases and choices allowed. After eliminating divisorial steps, special termination permits deletion of the entire boundary floor and reduction to strong generalized klt termination.
- **claim-nonempty-crepant-gdlt-lift** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-nonempty-lift)): Let $$X\xrightarrow{\ f\ }Z\xleftarrow{\ f^+\ }X^+$$ be any one of the small diagrams allowed in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, with normal globally Weil $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial compact Kähler fourfolds on the two sides, effective rational boundaries $B,B^+$ related by strict transform, the same rational nef b-divisor, and the compatible actual adjoints $D_X,D_{X^+}$. Assume $(X,B+\mathbf{M})$ is glc, and represent the b-divisor by an analytically nef $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor on a projective carrier over $X$. Suppose $$h:(Y,\Theta+\mathbf{M})\longrightarrow(X,B+\mathbf{M})$$ is a projective crepant gdlt modification as in Proposition <a href="#prop:gdlt-modification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:gdlt-modification">6</a>; in particular $Y$ is strong, $\Theta\geq0$, and every $h$-exceptional prime has coefficient one. Then there is a finite sequence of elementary negative gdlt steps over $Z$, of length $m\geq1$, $$(Y,\Theta+\mathbf{M})=(Y_0,\Theta_0+\mathbf{M}) \dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(Y_m,\Theta_m+\mathbf{M}),$$ and a projective bimeromorphic morphism $h^+:Y_m\to X^+$ such that $$K_{Y_m}+\Theta_m+M_{Y_m}=(h^+)^*D_{X^+}.$$ The end is a strong normal compact Kähler gdlt model with effective boundary, and every $h^+$-exceptional prime has coefficient one in $\Theta_m$. Thus it is again a crepant gdlt modification of the same type.
  - Explanation: Each prescribed downstairs flip lifts to a nonempty finite elementary generalized dlt program. Its endpoint maps projectively and crepantly to the positive downstairs model and again has coefficient-one exceptional primes; that morphism need not be an isomorphism.

There is $\\varepsilon>0$ such that every global place satisfies $$a(E;T,B+\\mathbf{M})\\geq\\varepsilon,$$ and only finitely many places exceptional over $T$ satisfy $$a(E;T,B+\\mathbf{M})<1+\\varepsilon.$$ This finite set can be represented simultaneously by prime divisors on a smooth model projective over $T$. In particular the exceptional places of discrepancy at most one form a finite set with such a realization.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-extraction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(T,B+\\mathbf{M})$ be rational gklt data on a strong normal compact Kähler space, with an absolutely nef rational line bundle on a projective carrier and rationally invertible adjoint. For any specified finite set $\\mathcal E$ of places exceptional over $T$ with $a(E;T,B+\\mathbf{M})\\leq1$, there is a projective bimeromorphic morphism $h:U\\to T$ such that $U$ is a strong normal compact Kähler space, the $h$-exceptional prime divisors are exactly the members of $\\mathcal E$, and the crepant generalized pair on $U$ is gklt with effective boundary. Thus, writing this boundary as $B_U$, $$K_U+B_U+M_U=h^*(K_T+B+M_T).$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-relative-program",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\pi:T\\to V$ be a projective bimeromorphic morphism of normal compact Kähler spaces. Suppose that $T$ is strong and that $(T,B+\\mathbf{M})$ is rational gdlt, with effective boundary, a projective carrier on which the rational line datum is nef over $V$, and a rationally invertible adjoint $D_T$.*\n\n1.  *If $D_T$ is not relatively nef over $V$, there is an elementary negative step over $V$. Its output is projective over $V$, normal compact Kähler and strong, and is gdlt with the transported boundary and b-data. The same construction is available after every finite prefix whose last adjoint is not relatively nef over $V$.*\n\n2.  *If the initial pair is gklt, there exists a finite, possibly empty, sequence of elementary negative steps over this one fixed base $V$ whose last adjoint is relatively nef over $V$. Its models and transported data have the properties in the first assertion.",

      },

Its output is projective over $V$, normal compact Kähler and strong, and is gdlt with the transported boundary and b-data. The same construction is available after every finite prefix whose last adjoint is not relatively nef over $V$.*\n\n2.  *If the initial pair is gklt, there exists a finite, possibly empty, sequence of elementary negative steps over this one fixed base $V$ whose last adjoint is relatively nef over $V$.         "exact_quote": "Let $(T,B+\\mathbf{M})$ be rational glc data of dimension at most four on a normal compact Kähler space.         "anchor": "stmt-thm-special-termination",
        "exact_quote": "Let $d\\leq4$, and let $$(T_0,B_0+\\mathbf{M})\\dashrightarrow(T_1,B_1+\\mathbf{M})\n \\dashrightarrow(T_2,B_2+\\mathbf{M})\\dashrightarrow\\cdots$$ be a sequence of elementary steps for rational gdlt data on strong normal compact Kähler spaces of dimension $d$. The boundaries are transported as in an elementary program, and the higher absolutely nef b-line datum is fixed and is carried by projective modifications. There is an index $i_0$ such that, for every $i\\geq i_0$, the exceptional loci on both sides of the step are disjoint from every generalized log canonical center on their respective sides.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-common-projective-models",
        "exact_quote": "Let a finite collection of normal irreducible compact Kähler spaces be joined, with compatible bimeromorphic identifications, by finitely many projective bimeromorphic morphisms and their inverses. Suppose that a fixed nef b-divisor is carried by a projective bimeromorphic morphism $X'\\to X$ to one of these spaces, with analytically nef $\\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier trace $M'$ on $X'$. Then there is a smooth compact Kähler space $W$ with compatible projective bimeromorphic morphisms to every space in the collection and to $X'$. The resolution may simultaneously principalize finitely many specified generically nonzero coherent ideals and resolve finitely many specified divisors. Its trace $M_W$ is the pullback of $M'$ and is analytically nef.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-common-projective-models",

Then there is a smooth compact Kähler space $W$ with compatible projective bimeromorphic morphisms to every space in the collection and to $X'$. The resolution may simultaneously principalize finitely many specified generically nonzero coherent ideals and resolve finitely many specified divisors. Its trace $M_W$ is the pullback of $M'$ and is analytically nef.",
          "source_id": "PM-056.1"
        }

Its trace $M_W$ is the pullback of $M'$ and is analytically nef.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a normal compact Kähler model, and let $r:W\\to T$ be a projective bimeromorphic morphism from a normal compact Kähler space $W$ dominating the fixed projective carrier of $\\mathbf{M}$. Assume that $M_T$ is $\\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier. Then $$\\label{eq:nef-defect}\n J_{W/T}:=r^*M_T-M_W\\geq0$$ is an exceptional $\\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor over $T$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ordinary-singularities",
        "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a normal compact Kähler space, let $B$ be an effective rational divisor of finite support, and let $\\mathbf{M}$ have analytically nef rational data on a projective carrier. Assume that $K_T$, $B$, and $M_T$ are $\\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier. If every global generalized log discrepancy of $(T,B+\\mathbf{M})$ is nonnegative, respectively positive, then the same condition holds for local analytic places. In the positive case, $T$ has ordinary klt singularities locally. If the global generalized discrepancies are positive and every global place exceptional over $T$ has generalized log discrepancy strictly greater than one, then $T$ has ordinary terminal singularities locally. In dimension four the latter conclusion implies $\\dim\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sing}}T\\leq1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.1"
      },

          "exact_quote": "If the global generalized discrepancies are positive and every global place exceptional over $T$ has generalized log discrepancy strictly greater than one, then $T$ has ordinary terminal singularities locally.",
          "source_id": "PM-056.1"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-ordinary-singularities"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-small-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Let $$T\\xrightarrow{\\ f\\ }Z\\xleftarrow{\\ f^+\\ }T^+$$ be a diagram of normal irreducible compact Kähler fourfolds in which $f$ and $f^+$ are projective small bimeromorphic morphisms with connected fibers, and both are nonisomorphisms. Let $(T,B_T+\\mathbf{M})$ and $(T^+,B_{T^+}+\\mathbf{M})$ have effective rational boundaries related by strict transform and the same fixed rational b-divisor. Transport the canonical representatives by the identification of prime divisors, and assume that the actual adjoints $D_T$ and $D_{T^+}$ are $\\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier, with $-D_T$ $f$-ample and $D_{T^+}$ $f^+$-ample.*\n\n\n\n*The exceptional images of $f$ and $f^+$ are equal. If their common image is $A$, then $$\\label{eq:small-loci}\n L:=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Exc}}(f)=f^{-1}(A),\\qquad\n L^+:=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Exc}}(f^+)=(f^+)^{-1}(A),$$ the complements are isomorphic, and $$\\label{eq:small-dimensions}\n \\dim L,\\dim L^+\\leq2,\\qquad \\dim A\\leq1,\n \\qquad \\dim L+\\dim L^+\\geq3.$$*\n\n\n*Let $G$ be the normalization of the main graph, with projections $p:G\\to T$, $q:G\\to T^+$ and $h=f\\circ p=f^+\\circ q$. Then the actual $\\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor $$\\label{eq:small-divisor}\n F:=p^*D_T-q^*D_{T^+}$$ is effective and exceptional over both $T$ and $T^+$, and $$\\label{eq:small-support}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Supp}}F=h^{-1}(A).$$ Moreover, $F\\cdot C<0$ for every irreducible curve $C$ contracted by $h$.*\n\n\n*For every global place $E$ over these spaces, let $\\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_E(F)$ denote the coefficient of the pullback of $F$ on any higher proper model dominating $G$ and carrying $E$. Then $$\\label{eq:small-discrepancies}\n a(E;T^+,B_{T^+}+\\mathbf{M})-a(E;T,B_T+\\mathbf{M})=\\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_E(F)\\geq0.$$ The inequality is strict exactly when $\\mathop{\\mathrm{cent}}_T(E)\\subset L$, and this condition is equivalent to $\\mathop{\\mathrm{cent}}_{T^+}(E)\\subset L^+$. A place is exceptional over $T$ if and only if it is exceptional over $T^+$.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-program-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y_0\\dashrightarrow\\cdots\\dashrightarrow Y_m$, with $m\\geq0$, be a finite sequence of normal irreducible compact Kähler fourfolds with $\\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier adjoints $D_j=K_{Y_j}+B_j+M_{Y_j}$. Suppose the effective rational boundaries are transported by pushforward, the canonical representatives are compatible, and the same b-divisor with a projective carrier is used throughout. Assume each step is either a small diagram satisfying Lemma <a href=\"#lem:small-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:small-comparison\">11</a>, or a projective bimeromorphic divisorial contraction $v_j:Y_j\\to Y_{j+1}$ such that $D_{j+1}=(v_j)_*D_j$ and $-D_j$ is $v_j$-ample. These steps extract no prime divisors. On a smooth common model $W$ with projective maps to every $Y_j$ and to the carrier, write $r_j:W\\to Y_j$. Then $$\\label{eq:program-comparison}\n r_0^*D_0-r_m^*D_m=E,\\qquad E\\geq0,\\qquad (r_m)_*E=0.$$ Every global discrepancy is nondecreasing along the run. If a prime divisor is contracted in a divisorial step, its discrepancy increases strictly at that step, and its discrepancy at the end is strictly larger than before that step.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-nef-ample",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y_0$, $Y$, and $T_+$ be normal irreducible compact Kähler spaces with projective bimeromorphic morphisms to a normal compact Kähler space $S$. On a smooth common model $W$ over $S$, projective over these spaces, let $P_0$, $P$, and $P_+$ be the pullbacks of respective $\\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisors $D_0$, $D_Y$, and $D_+$. Suppose that $$P_0=P+E_Y=P_++E_+,$$ where $E_Y\\geq0$ is exceptional over $Y$ and $E_+\\geq0$ is exceptional over $T_+$. If $D_Y$ is nef over $S$ and $D_+$ is ample over $S$, then $P=P_+$ as actual divisors. The induced bimeromorphic map is a projective morphism $\\varphi:Y\\to T_+$ over $S$, and $D_Y=\\varphi^*D_+$.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-nef-ample",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Y_0$, $Y$, and $T_+$ be normal irreducible compact Kähler spaces with projective bimeromorphic morphisms to a normal compact Kähler space $S$. Suppose that $$P_0=P+E_Y=P_++E_+,$$ where $E_Y\\geq0$ is exceptional over $Y$ and $E_+\\geq0$ is exceptional over $T_+$. If $D_Y$ is nef over $S$ and $D_+$ is ample over $S$, then $P=P_+$ as actual divisors. The induced bimeromorphic map is a projective morphism $\\varphi:Y\\to T_+$ over $S$, and $D_Y=\\varphi^*D_+$.",
          "source_id": "PM-056.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $Y_0$, $Y$, and $T_+$ be normal irreducible compact Kähler spaces with projective bimeromorphic morphisms to a normal compact Kähler space $S$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cycle-loss",
        "exact_quote": "Let $k\\geq0$ be an integer, and let $X\\dashrightarrow Y$ be a bimeromorphic transformation of compact Kähler spaces represented by a proper bimeromorphic diagram. Suppose it identifies $$U=X\\setminus A\\simeq Y\\setminus B,$$ where $A$ and $B$ are closed analytic subspaces and $\\dim B<k$. Then restriction to $U$ and strict transform induce a surjection $\\mathcal C_k(X)\\to\\mathcal C_k(Y)$. Consequently $c_k(X)\\geq c_k(Y)$, with strict inequality if $A$ contains an irreducible compact analytic subspace of dimension $k$.*\n\n*In a sequence of bimeromorphic transformations of normal irreducible $n$-dimensional compact Kähler spaces which extract no prime divisors, only finitely many transformations can contract a prime divisor; a small transformation preserves $c_{n-1}$. In particular, for a small fourfold diagram of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:small-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:small-comparison\">11</a>, if $\\dim L^+<2$, then $c_2(T)\\geq c_2(T^+)$, strictly if $L$ contains a surface.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cycle-loss",
          "exact_quote": "Let $k\\geq0$ be an integer, and let $X\\dashrightarrow Y$ be a bimeromorphic transformation of compact Kähler spaces represented by a proper bimeromorphic diagram. Suppose it identifies $$U=X\\setminus A\\simeq Y\\setminus B,$$ where $A$ and $B$ are closed analytic subspaces and $\\dim B<k$. Then restriction to $U$ and strict transform induce a surjection $\\mathcal C_k(X)\\to\\mathcal C_k(Y)$. Consequently $c_k(X)\\geq c_k(Y)$, with strict inequality if $A$ contains an irreducible compact analytic subspace of dimension $k$.*\n\n*In a sequence of bimeromorphic transformations of normal irreducible $n$-dimensional compact Kähler spaces which extract no prime divisors, only finitely many transformations can contract a prime divisor; a small transformation preserves $c_{n-1}$. In particular, for a small fourfold diagram of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:small-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:small-comparison\">11</a>, if $\\dim L^+<2$, then $c_2(T)\\geq c_2(T^+)$, strictly if $L$ contains a surface.",

      "exact_statement": "Let $k\\geq0$ be an integer, and let $X\\dashrightarrow Y$ be a bimeromorphic transformation of compact Kähler spaces represented by a proper bimeromorphic diagram. Consequently $c_k(X)\\geq c_k(Y)$, with strict inequality if $A$ contains an irreducible compact analytic subspace of dimension $k$.*\n\n*In a sequence of bimeromorphic transformations of normal irreducible $n$-dimensional compact Kähler spaces which extract no prime divisors, only finitely many transformations can contract a prime divisor; a small transformation preserves $c_{n-1}$. In particular, for a small fourfold diagram of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:small-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:small-comparison\">11</a>, if $\\dim L^+<2$, then $c_2(T)\\geq c_2(T^+)$, strictly if $L$ contains a surface.",

Suppose it identifies $$U=X\\setminus A\\simeq Y\\setminus B,$$ where $A$ and $B$ are closed analytic subspaces and $\\dim B<k$.",
          "source_id": "PM-056.1"

      ],
      "id": "claim-analytic-cycle-rank-loss",
      "kind": "bound",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-terminal-junctions",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that the sequence above is infinite. After passing to a tail, there are a number $\\varepsilon>0$ and a finite set $\\mathcal I$ of global places such that, at every remaining index $i$, $$\\label{eq:stable-low-places}\n \\begin{gathered}\n a_i(E)\\geq\\varepsilon\\quad\\text{for every global place }E,\n \\\\\n \\mathcal I=\n \\{E:E\\text{ is exceptional over }X_i,\\ a_i(E)\\leq 1\\}.\n \\end{gathered}$$ There are projective bimeromorphic crepant morphisms $$\\label{eq:terminal-junction}\n \\begin{gathered}\n h_i:(Y_i,\\Theta_i+\\mathbf{M})\\longrightarrow (X_i,B_i+\\mathbf{M}),\n \\\\\n K_{Y_i}+\\Theta_i+M_{Y_i}=h_i^*D_i,\n \\end{gathered}$$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Each $Y_i$ is a strong normal irreducible compact Kähler fourfold, $\\Theta_i$ is effective, and $(Y_i,\\Theta_i+\\mathbf{M})$ is generalized terminal. The exceptional prime divisors of $h_i$ represent exactly the places in $\\mathcal I$.*\n\n\n2.  *For $E\\in\\mathcal I$, let $S_{i,E}$ be its prime on $Y_i$. Define $$\\label{eq:junction-coefficient-change}\n     \\Theta_i'=\n     \\Theta_i-\\sum_{E\\in\\mathcal I}\n           \\bigl(a_{i+1}(E)-a_i(E)\\bigr)S_{i,E}.$$ Starting with $(Y_i,\\Theta_i'+\\mathbf{M})$, a finite, possibly empty, sequence of small elementary steps over $Z_i$ ends at $(Y_{i+1},\\Theta_{i+1}+\\mathbf{M})$. All its models are strong normal irreducible compact Kähler fourfolds, all its pairs are generalized terminal, and its boundary and nef data are transported as in an elementary program. In particular, every global place is exceptional over one model of this connecting sequence if and only if it is exceptional over all of them.*\n\n3.  *There is one finite label set $\\mathcal H$ for the primes supplied by $\\mathcal I$ and by the components of $B_0$. Write $S_h$ for the current strict transform of a label and $b_h$ for its current coefficient. Labels are retained when their coefficient is zero. On the entire chain of coefficient changes and small steps, $$\\Theta=\\sum_{h\\in\\mathcal H}b_hS_h,\n                        \\qquad 0\\leq b_h<1,$$ and every $b_h$ is nonincreasing. Call a label *varying* if its coefficient sequence is not eventually constant, and *fixed* otherwise. After a further tail is chosen, all fixed coefficients are constant. Every varying label comes from $\\mathcal I$ and has positive coefficient at every remaining stage.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-terminal-junctions",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that the sequence above is infinite. After passing to a tail, there are a number $\\varepsilon>0$ and a finite set $\\mathcal I$ of global places such that, at every remaining index $i$, $$\\label{eq:stable-low-places}\n \\begin{gathered}\n a_i(E)\\geq\\varepsilon\\quad\\text{for every global place }E,\n \\\\\n \\mathcal I=\n \\{E:E\\text{ is exceptional over }X_i,\\ a_i(E)\\leq 1\\}.\n \\end{gathered}$$ There are projective bimeromorphic crepant morphisms $$\\label{eq:terminal-junction}\n \\begin{gathered}\n h_i:(Y_i,\\Theta_i+\\mathbf{M})\\longrightarrow (X_i,B_i+\\mathbf{M}),\n \\\\\n K_{Y_i}+\\Theta_i+M_{Y_i}=h_i^*D_i,\n \\end{gathered}$$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Each $Y_i$ is a strong normal irreducible compact Kähler fourfold, $\\Theta_i$ is effective, and $(Y_i,\\Theta_i+\\mathbf{M})$ is generalized terminal. The exceptional prime divisors of $h_i$ represent exactly the places in $\\mathcal I$.*\n\n\n2.  *For $E\\in\\mathcal I$, let $S_{i,E}$ be its prime on $Y_i$. Define $$\\label{eq:junction-coefficient-change}\n     \\Theta_i'=\n     \\Theta_i-\\sum_{E\\in\\mathcal I}\n           \\bigl(a_{i+1}(E)-a_i(E)\\bigr)S_{i,E}.$$ Starting with $(Y_i,\\Theta_i'+\\mathbf{M})$, a finite, possibly empty, sequence of small elementary steps over $Z_i$ ends at $(Y_{i+1},\\Theta_{i+1}+\\mathbf{M})$. All its models are strong normal irreducible compact Kähler fourfolds, all its pairs are generalized terminal, and its boundary and nef data are transported as in an elementary program. In particular, every global place is exceptional over one model of this connecting sequence if and only if it is exceptional over all of them.*\n\n3.  *There is one finite label set $\\mathcal H$ for the primes supplied by $\\mathcal I$ and by the components of $B_0$. Write $S_h$ for the current strict transform of a label and $b_h$ for its current coefficient. Labels are retained when their coefficient is zero. On the entire chain of coefficient changes and small steps, $$\\Theta=\\sum_{h\\in\\mathcal H}b_hS_h,\n                        \\qquad 0\\leq b_h<1,$$ and every $b_h$ is nonincreasing. Call a label *varying* if its coefficient sequence is not eventually constant, and *fixed* otherwise. After a further tail is chosen, all fixed coefficients are constant. Every varying label comes from $\\mathcal I$ and has positive coefficient at every remaining stage.",
          "source_id": "PM-056.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Suppose that the sequence above is infinite. After passing to a tail, there are a number $\\varepsilon>0$ and a finite set $\\mathcal I$ of global places such that, at every remaining index $i$, $$\\label{eq:stable-low-places}\n \\begin{gathered}\n a_i(E)\\geq\\varepsilon\\quad\\text{for every global place }E,\n \\\\\n \\mathcal I=\n \\{E:E\\text{ is exceptional over }X_i,\\ a_i(E)\\leq 1\\}.\n \\end{gathered}$$ There are projective bimeromorphic crepant morphisms $$\\label{eq:terminal-junction}\n \\begin{gathered}\n h_i:(Y_i,\\Theta_i+\\mathbf{M})\\longrightarrow (X_i,B_i+\\mathbf{M}),\n \\\\\n K_{Y_i}+\\Theta_i+M_{Y_i}=h_i^*D_i,\n \\end{gathered}$$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Each $Y_i$ is a strong normal irreducible compact Kähler fourfold, $\\Theta_i$ is effective, and $(Y_i,\\Theta_i+\\mathbf{M})$ is generalized terminal. The exceptional prime divisors of $h_i$ represent exactly the places in $\\mathcal I$.*\n\n\n2.  *For $E\\in\\mathcal I$, let $S_{i,E}$ be its prime on $Y_i$. Define $$\\label{eq:junction-coefficient-change}\n     \\Theta_i'=\n     \\Theta_i-\\sum_{E\\in\\mathcal I}\n           \\bigl(a_{i+1}(E)-a_i(E)\\bigr)S_{i,E}.$$ Starting with $(Y_i,\\Theta_i'+\\mathbf{M})$, a finite, possibly empty, sequence of small elementary steps over $Z_i$ ends at $(Y_{i+1},\\Theta_{i+1}+\\mathbf{M})$. All its models are strong normal irreducible compact Kähler fourfolds, all its pairs are generalized terminal, and its boundary and nef data are transported as in an elementary program. In particular, every global place is exceptional over one model of this connecting sequence if and only if it is exceptional over all of them.*\n\n3.  *There is one finite label set $\\mathcal H$ for the primes supplied by $\\mathcal I$ and by the components of $B_0$. Write $S_h$ for the current strict transform of a label and $b_h$ for its current coefficient. Labels are retained when their coefficient is zero. On the entire chain of coefficient changes and small steps, $$\\Theta=\\sum_{h\\in\\mathcal H}b_hS_h,\n                        \\qquad 0\\leq b_h<1,$$ and every $b_h$ is nonincreasing. Call a label *varying* if its coefficient sequence is not eventually constant, and *fixed* otherwise. After a further tail is chosen, all fixed coefficients are constant. Every varying label comes from $\\mathcal I$ and has positive coefficient at every remaining stage.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-surface-finiteness",
        "exact_quote": "In the setting of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:terminal-junctions\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:terminal-junctions\">15</a>, there is a finite set $$\\mathcal A_{\\mathrm{surf}}\n                    \\subset [\\varepsilon,1]\\cap\\mathbb{Q}$$ such that $a_i(E)\\in\\mathcal A_{\\mathrm{surf}}$ whenever $E\\in\\mathcal I$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{cent}}_{X_i}(E)$ is a surface. After passing to a further tail, the following conclusions hold.*\n\n1.  *No $E\\in\\mathcal I$ has a surface center contained in $L_i$ on $X_i$, or a surface center contained in $L_i^+$ on $X_{i+1}$.*\n\n2.  *Every varying label $S_h\\subset Y_i$ satisfies $\\dim h_i(S_h)\\leq1$ at every junction.*\n\n3.  *For an irreducible compact analytic surface contained in either $L_i$ or $L_i^+$, the corresponding junction morphism is an isomorphism near its general point, and the downstairs model is smooth there. Blowing up the coherent ideal of that surface defines a unique global generic blowup place.         "anchor": "stmt-thm-branch",
        "exact_quote": "Let $p\\colon Y\\to X$ be a projective bimeromorphic morphism of normal irreducible compact Kähler fourfolds, where $Y$ has ordinary terminal singularities. Let $Q=\\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}Q_j$ be a reduced union of distinct irreducible divisors satisfying $\\dim p(Q)\\leq1$. Then the sum defining $\\beta(Q)$ and the count defining $\\tau_Y(Q)$ are finite, and $$\\label{eq:branch-inequality}\n \\beta(Q)\\leq\\sum_{j=1}^{m}c_2(Q_j^\\nu)+\\tau_Y(Q).$$ The rank $c_2(Q_j^\\nu)$ is the rational rank spanned in $H_4(Q_j^\\nu,\\mathbb{Q})$ by the fundamental classes of compact irreducible analytic surfaces.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-branch",

          "source_id": "PM-056.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $p\\colon Y\\to X$ be a projective bimeromorphic morphism of normal irreducible compact Kähler fourfolds, where $Y$ has ordinary terminal singularities.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-link-quotient",
        "exact_quote": "For every singular curve $T$ with the choices in <a href=\"#eq:branch-local-systems\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:branch-local-systems\">[eq:branch-local-systems]</a>, restriction from the resolution to the regular locus induces a surjection of local systems $$\\label{eq:branch-link-quotient}\n \\mathcal W_T\\twoheadrightarrow\\mathcal V_T.$$ For every $t\\in T^\\circ$, its map on fibers is equivariant for the action of $\\pi_1(T^\\circ,t)$ and is the map induced by restriction $$\\label{eq:branch-link-stalk-map}\n \\begin{aligned}\n (\\mathcal W_T)_t\n &\\simeq H^2(g^{-1}(t))\n  \\simeq\\varinjlim_{U\\ni t}H^2(g^{-1}U)\\\\\n &\\longrightarrow\\varinjlim_{U\\ni t}H^2(U\\cap Y_{\\mathrm{reg}})\n  \\simeq(\\mathcal V_T)_t,\n \\end{aligned}$$ where $U$ runs through sufficiently small open neighborhoods of $t$ in $Y$ and $g^{-1}(U\\cap Y_{\\mathrm{reg}})$ is identified with $U\\cap Y_{\\mathrm{reg}}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-link-quotient",
          "exact_quote": "For every singular curve $T$ with the choices in <a href=\"#eq:branch-local-systems\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:branch-local-systems\">[eq:branch-local-systems]</a>, restriction from the resolution to the regular locus induces a surjection of local systems $$\\label{eq:branch-link-quotient}\n \\mathcal W_T\\twoheadrightarrow\\mathcal V_T.$$ For every $t\\in T^\\circ$, its map on fibers is equivariant for the action of $\\pi_1(T^\\circ,t)$ and is the map induced by restriction $$\\label{eq:branch-link-stalk-map}\n \\begin{aligned}\n (\\mathcal W_T)_t\n &\\simeq H^2(g^{-1}(t))\n  \\simeq\\varinjlim_{U\\ni t}H^2(g^{-1}U)\\\\\n &\\longrightarrow\\varinjlim_{U\\ni t}H^2(U\\cap Y_{\\mathrm{reg}})\n  \\simeq(\\mathcal V_T)_t,\n \\end{aligned}$$ where $U$ runs through sufficiently small open neighborhoods of $t$ in $Y$ and $g^{-1}(U\\cap Y_{\\mathrm{reg}})$ is identified with $U\\cap Y_{\\mathrm{reg}}$.",

      "exact_statement": "For every singular curve $T$ with the choices in <a href=\"#eq:branch-local-systems\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:branch-local-systems\">[eq:branch-local-systems]</a>, restriction from the resolution to the regular locus induces a surjection of local systems $$\\label{eq:branch-link-quotient}\n \\mathcal W_T\\twoheadrightarrow\\mathcal V_T.$$ For every $t\\in T^\\circ$, its map on fibers is equivariant for the action of $\\pi_1(T^\\circ,t)$ and is the map induced by restriction $$\\label{eq:branch-link-stalk-map}\n \\begin{aligned}\n (\\mathcal W_T)_t\n &\\simeq H^2(g^{-1}(t))\n  \\simeq\\varinjlim_{U\\ni t}H^2(g^{-1}U)\\\\\n &\\longrightarrow\\varinjlim_{U\\ni t}H^2(U\\cap Y_{\\mathrm{reg}})\n  \\simeq(\\mathcal V_T)_t,\n \\end{aligned}$$ where $U$ runs through sufficiently small open neighborhoods of $t$ in $Y$ and $g^{-1}(U\\cap Y_{\\mathrm{reg}})$ is identified with $U\\cap Y_{\\mathrm{reg}}$.",
      "explanation": "Resolution-fiber cohomology surjects naturally onto punctured-neighborhood cohomology along each singular curve. The stalk restriction maps respect monodromy.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-curve-places",
        "exact_quote": "For every irreducible curve component $T$ of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sing}}Y$, with $b_T$ as in <a href=\"#eq:branch-local-systems\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:branch-local-systems\">[eq:branch-local-systems]</a> and the fixed resolution $g:\\widetilde Y\\to Y$, $$b_T\\ \\le\\\n \\#\\left\\{\n \\begin{array}{c}\n E\\text{ a prime component of }\\mathop{\\mathrm{Exc}}(g):\\\\\n g(E)=T,\\quad a(E;Y,0)=2\n \\end{array}\n \\right\\}.$$ In particular, the divisors counted here represent distinct global divisorial places.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.1"
      },

        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-difficulty-finite",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(Y,\\Theta+\\mathbf{M})$ be a model of the truncated terminal chain. Among the places exceptional over $Y$ with discrepancy less than two, there are only finitely many whose centers have codimension at least three. For each fixed irreducible compact analytic surface $V\\subset Y$, there are only finitely many whose center is $V$.*\n\n*There is a finite set $\\mathcal V_Y$ of irreducible compact analytic surfaces with the following additional property. If $V\\notin\\mathcal V_Y$, its general point lies in a smooth open where $\\mathbf{M}$ descends and either no label occurs or exactly one smooth label $S_h$ occurs. There are no positive-weight places centered on $V$ in the first case or when $b_h=0$. When $b_h>0$, they are exactly the echoes $E_{V,h,j}$ with $j(1-b_h)<1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-difficulty-ranks",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a small step of the terminal chain, with exceptional loci $L,L^+$, $$Y\\xrightarrow{f}Z\\xleftarrow{f^+}Y^+.$$ For each label $h$, let $e_h$ and $e_h^+$ be the numbers of prime divisors in $S_h^\\nu$ and $(S_h^+)^\\nu$ lying over $L$ and $L^+$, respectively. Then these numbers are finite and $$\\label{eq:difficulty-rank-change}\n \\begin{gathered}\n e_h=\\sum_{\\substack{V\\subset L\\\\\\dim V=2}}r_{V,h},\\qquad\n e_h^+=\\sum_{\\substack{V^+\\subset L^+\\\\\\dim V^+=2}}r_{V^+,h}^+,\\\\\n c_2(S_h^\\nu)-c_2((S_h^+)^\\nu)=e_h-e_h^+.\n \\end{gathered}$$ The surface sums run over irreducible compact analytic surfaces.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-difficulty-change",
        "exact_quote": "The difficulty is nonincreasing under every coefficient change and every small step of the truncated terminal chain. More precisely:*\n\n\n1.  *Suppose $\\Theta'\\leq\\Theta$ is a coefficient change on the same model $Y$, and write $w(E)$ and $w'(E)$ for its old and new weights. The set $$\\mathcal C=\\{E:\\ E\\text{ exceptional over }Y,\\ w(E)\\ne w'(E)\\}$$ is finite, and $$\\label{eq:difficulty-coefficient-change}\n     \\mathfrak D(Y,\\Theta+\\mathbf{M})-\\mathfrak D(Y,\\Theta'+\\mathbf{M})\n     =\\sum_{E\\in\\mathcal C}\\bigl(w(E)-w'(E)\\bigr)\\geq0.$$*\n\n\n2.  *For a small step $Y\\to Z\\leftarrow Y^+$ with exceptional loci $L,L^+$, write $w(E),w^+(E)$ for the two weights. The set $$\\mathcal P=\\{E:\\ E\\text{ exceptional over }Y,\\\n           \\mathop{\\mathrm{cent}}_Y(E)\\subset L,\\ w(E)+w^+(E)>0\\}$$ is finite. Its center condition is equivalently $\\mathop{\\mathrm{cent}}_{Y^+}(E)\\subset L^+$, and $$\\label{eq:difficulty-small-change}\n     \\mathfrak D(Y,\\Theta+\\mathbf{M})-\\mathfrak D(Y^+,\\Theta^++\\mathbf{M})\n     =\\sum_{E\\in\\mathcal P}\\bigl(w(E)-w^+(E)\\bigr)\\geq0.$$*\n\n*In either move, a strict decrease in the weight of any exceptional place forces a strict decrease in the difficulty.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-difficulty-change",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Global strong Q-factoriality** ([source](#stmt-def-strong-factoriality)): A normal compact complex space $T$ is *globally strongly $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial* if, for every coherent rank-one reflexive sheaf $\mathcal F$ on all of $T$, some positive reflexive power $$\mathcal F^{[m]}=(\mathcal F^{\otimes m})^{**},\qquad m>0,$$ is an invertible sheaf.
- **Global Weil Q-factoriality** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A normal compact complex space $X$ is *globally Weil $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial* if every prime Weil divisor defined on all of $X$ is $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier and some positive reflexive power of its canonical sheaf is invertible.
- **Analytically nef rational b-divisor** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a normal compact Kähler space $X$, a rational nef b-divisor $\mathbf{M}$ in this paper is represented by a $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor $M'$ on a normal compact Kähler space $X'$ with a projective bimeromorphic map $\pi:X'\to X$. We require $c_1(M')$ to lie in the closure of the Kähler cone in real Bott–Chern cohomology.
- **Global divisorial place** ([source](#eq:discrepancy-definition)): A *global divisorial place* is a prime divisor on a normal proper bimeromorphic model, with primes identified by strict transform on common higher models.
- **Generalized log discrepancy** ([source](#eq:discrepancy-definition)): Its log discrepancy is $$a(E;X,B+\mathbf{M})=1-\operatorname{coeff}_E(\Delta_W)$$ on a model carrying $E$.
- **Generalized log canonical pair** ([source](#eq:discrepancy-definition)): The pair is *generalized log canonical* if this number is nonnegative for every global divisorial place, including the primes on $X$.
- **Generalized klt and generalized lc** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): A pair is gklt if all of its global log discrepancies are positive, and glc if they are all nonnegative.
- **Generalized terminal pair** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): It is *generalized terminal* if it is gklt and $a(E;T,B+\mathbf{M})>1$ for every place exceptional over $T$.
- **Generalized log canonical center** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): A *generalized log canonical center* is the center of a global place of log discrepancy zero.
- **Generalized dlt pair** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): A glc pair is gdlt if there is a Zariski-open set $T^\circ\subset T$ on which $(T^\circ,B\|_{T^\circ})$ is simple normal crossing and the b-data descend, and which contains the general point of every generalized log canonical center.
- **Rational line bundle** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): A *rational line bundle* on $T$ is an element of $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(T)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}$.
- **Relative nefness** ([source](#sec:comparisons)): For a projective morphism, a rational Cartier divisor is called *relatively nef* here if it has nonnegative degree on every compact irreducible curve contracted by the morphism.
- **Small morphism** ([source](#stmt-thm-main)): Here *small* means that the exceptional locus contains no prime divisor.
- **Effective nef defect** ([source](#stmt-lem-nef-defect)): Then $$\label{eq:nef-defect} J_{W/T}:=r^*M_T-M_W\geq0$$ is an exceptional $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor over $T$.
- **Branch count and total branch excess** ([source](#eq:branch-count-definition)): For an irreducible compact analytic surface $V\subset Q$, define $$\label{eq:branch-count-definition} \begin{aligned} r_V(Q)&=\#\{F:\ F\text{ is a prime divisor of }\coprod_jQ_j^\nu, \ \nu(F)=V\},\\ \beta(Q)&=\sum_{\substack{V\subset Q\\ \dim V=2}}(r_V(Q)-1). \end{aligned}$$
- **Global singular-curve place count** ([source](#eq:branch-place-count)): Put $$\label{eq:branch-place-count} \tau_Y(Q)=\#\left\{ \begin{array}{@{}l@{}} E\text{ a global place over }Y:\\[-2pt] \mathop{\mathrm{cent}}_Y(E)\text{ is an irreducible curve contained in }\mathop{\mathrm{Sing}}Y\cap Q, \quad a(E;Y,0)=2 \end{array}\right\}.$$
- **Ceiling weight and exceptional weight** ([source](#sec:difficulty)): For $u\in\mathbb{R}$, put $$\label{eq:difficulty-weights} \omega(u)=\left\lceil N\max\{u,0\}\right\rceil, \qquad w(E)=\omega\bigl(2-a(E;Y,\Theta+\mathbf{M})\bigr),$$ where $E$ is a global divisorial place and the current pair is understood in $w(E)$.
- **Echo default contribution** ([source](#pm-h-18)): Define the *default contribution* of label $h$ by the finite sum $$\label{eq:echo-default} d_h=\sum_{j\geq1}\omega\bigl(1-j(1-b_h)\bigr).$$
- **Local surface bracket** ([source](#eq:difficulty-bracket)): Define the integer $$\label{eq:difficulty-bracket} q_Y(V)=\sum_{\mathop{\mathrm{cent}}_Y(E)=V}w(E)-\sum_{h\in\mathcal H}r_{V,h}d_h.$$
- **Difficulty** ([source](#eq:difficulty)): We may therefore define the difficulty by $$\label{eq:difficulty} \begin{aligned} \mathfrak D(Y,\Theta+\mathbf{M}) ={}&\sum_{\mathop{\mathrm{codim}}_Y\mathop{\mathrm{cent}}_Y(E)\geq3}w(E) +\sum_{\substack{V\subset Y\\\dim V=2}}q_Y(V)\\ &+\sum_{h\in\mathcal H}d_hc_2(S_h^\nu), \end{aligned}$$ where the surface sum is over irreducible compact analytic surfaces.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-global-discrepancy-floor | a(E;T,B+\mathbf{M}) | ≥ | $\varepsilon$ | The positive lower bound belongs to this pair. |
| q-low-place-threshold | a(E;T,B+\mathbf{M}) | < | $1+\varepsilon$ | The threshold specifies the finite set of exceptional places in the proposition. |
| q-terminal-singular-locus | \dim\mathop{\mathrm{Sing}}T | ≤ | $1$ | The singular locus of the current terminal model. |
| q-small-exceptional-loci-dimension | \dim L,\dim L^+ | ≤ | $2$ | Both exceptional loci in the given diagram. |
| q-small-exceptional-image-dimension | \dim A | ≤ | $1$ | The image in the normal compact Kähler base. |
| q-small-exceptional-dimension-sum | \dim L+\dim L^+ | ≥ | $3$ | The sum of dimensions of its exceptional loci. |
| q-transverse-self-intersection-ceiling | u_j | ≤ | $\left\lceil\frac2\varepsilon\right\rceil$ | Uniform over the junctions and marked surfaces of the fixed strong generalized klt sequence. |
| q-transverse-row-intersection-bound | \sum_{k\ne j}m_{jk} | < | $U^2$ | Each row of the matrix in the marked-surface calculation. |
| q-branch-excess-ceiling | \beta(Q) | ≤ | $\sum_{j=1}^{m}c_2(Q_j^\nu)+\tau_Y(Q)$ | The union has image of dimension at most one under the projective bimeromorphic morphism. |
| q-echo-log-discrepancy | a(E_{V,h,j};Y,\Theta+\mathbf{M}) | = | $1+j(1-b_h)$ | The successive echo indices for the fixed label and surface. |
| q-echo-default | d_h | = | $\sum_{j\geq1}\omega\bigl(1-j(1-b_h)\bigr)$ | The current label coefficient; defaults are later made constant on a tail. |
| q-junction-difficulty-floor | \mathfrak D(Y_i,\Theta_i+\mathbf{M}) | ≥ | $0$ | Junctions only; intermediate models are not asserted to satisfy this lower bound. |
| q-positive-detector-weight-loss | w_i(E) | ≥ | $w_{i+1}(E)+1$ | Weights at the adjacent crepant junctions with the chosen denominator. |
| q-gdlt-lift-nonempty | m | ≥ | $1$ | The number of elementary steps in the finite lift. |
