<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-low-place-input** [claim](#stmt-prop-low-places) / [proof](#stmt-prop-low-places): Use the recalled rational generalized klt low-place result. -> A positive discrepancy floor and a finite projectively realizable low-place set.
- **step-extraction-input** [claim](#stmt-prop-extraction) / [proof](#stmt-prop-extraction): Use the recalled exact crepant extraction result on a strong model. -> A projective crepant extraction with exactly the specified exceptional primes.
- **step-relative-program-input** [claim](#stmt-prop-relative-program) / [proof](#stmt-prop-relative-program): Distinguish relative generalized dlt continuation from existence of a finite generalized klt nef run. -> Continuation at non-nef prefixes and a finite nef run in the generalized klt case.
- **step-gdlt-modification-input** [claim](#stmt-prop-gdlt-modification) / [proof](#stmt-prop-gdlt-modification): Use the recalled crepant generalized dlt modification theorem. -> A strong crepant generalized dlt model with coefficient-one exceptional primes.
- **step-special-termination-input** [claim](#stmt-thm-special-termination) / [proof](#stmt-thm-special-termination): Use special termination for elementary generalized dlt sequences on strong models. -> A tail whose exceptional loci avoid all generalized log canonical centers.
- **step-common-projective-model** [claim](#stmt-lem-common-projective-models) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-common-projective-models): Normalize dominating fiber-product components, then resolve and principalize projectively. -> A smooth compact Kähler common model with analytically nef pulled-back trace.
- **step-effective-nef-defect** [claim](#stmt-lem-nef-defect) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-nef-defect): Apply analytic negativity to the trace difference using the relative nefness of the carrier pullback. -> An effective exceptional nef defect compatible with higher pullback.
- **step-local-ordinary-singularities** [claim](#stmt-lem-ordinary-singularities) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ordinary-singularities): Compare simple normal crossing generalized boundaries with ordinary boundaries using effective boundary pullback and nef defect. -> Local generalized inequalities and ordinary klt or terminal singularities as appropriate.
- **step-small-graph-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-small-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-small-comparison): Compare isomorphism opens, apply graph negativity, and use negative curve degrees to identify the full correction support. -> Equal exceptional images, dimensional constraints, and the exact discrepancy strictness locus.
- **step-finite-program-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-program-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-program-comparison): Pull back and sum effective step corrections; use absence of extraction to retain exceptionality at the endpoint. -> An effective endpoint-exceptional accumulated correction and persistent discrepancy improvement.
- **step-nef-ample-identification** [claim](#stmt-lem-nef-ample) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-nef-ample): Apply negativity in both signs, then descend the map through connected projective fibers. -> Equal adjoint pullbacks and a projective crepant factorization to the ample model.
- **step-cycle-rank-loss** [claim](#stmt-lem-cycle-loss) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cycle-loss): Use Borel–Moore localization and nonvanishing of compact analytic cycle classes on Kähler spaces. -> A surjection of cycle spans and strict rank loss for a removed source cycle.
- **step-terminal-junction-chain** [claim](#stmt-prop-terminal-junctions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-terminal-junctions): Stabilize the finite decreasing low-place sets, extract them, adjust coefficients and connect by finite relative nef runs. -> Crepant generalized terminal junctions with finite small connectors and persistent nonincreasing labels.
- **step-marked-surface-lattice** [claim](#stmt-prop-surface-finiteness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-surface-finiteness): Slice transversely, bound negative-definite intersection rows, group by global labels and apply a nonsingular minor with a fixed denominator. -> A finite set of marked surface discrepancies and a tail where varying labels have only curve or point images.
- **step-normalization-excision** [claim](#stmt-lem-branch-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-branch-normalization): Use normalization excision, top surface homology, weight strictness and duality. -> Finite branch excess bounded after cycle-rank subtraction by the weight-graded restriction rank.
- **step-analytic-support-decomposition** [claim](#stmt-lem-branch-analytic-decomposition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-branch-analytic-decomposition): Globalize relative hard Lefschetz, apply formal splitting and glue strict-support projectors. -> Finite global perverse decompositions with unique full-support intersection complexes.
- **step-resolved-cohomology-purity** [claim](#stmt-lem-branch-resolved-purity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-branch-resolved-purity): Bound support shifts, identify supported contributions, and prove filtered restriction through a projective hyperresolution. -> Surjective restriction and purity in the stated cohomological degree.
- **step-singular-curve-cone** [claim](#stmt-lem-branch-cone) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-branch-cone): Use the cone of the constant-to-intersection-complex map and stratified compactly supported cohomology. -> The weight-graded restriction rank is bounded by singular-curve contributions.
- **step-local-system-quotient** [claim](#stmt-lem-link-quotient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-link-quotient): Factor the canonical regular-locus comparison through the full-support summand and use the attaching truncation. -> A natural monodromy-equivariant quotient from resolution-fiber cohomology.
- **step-global-curve-place-realization** [claim](#stmt-prop-curve-places) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-curve-places): Establish finite monodromy, realize invariant classes near a loop-carrying compact core, use local small factorialization and fiber degrees, then compare discrepancies globally. -> Distinct global discrepancy-two divisors numbering at least the curve contribution.
- **step-branch-inequality** [claim](#stmt-thm-branch) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-curve-places-2): Combine normalization, purity, cone and global curve-place bounds; represent all counted places on the compact resolution. -> Finite branch excess and finite global curve-place count satisfying the branch inequality.
- **step-low-weight-finiteness** [claim](#stmt-prop-difficulty-finite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-difficulty-finite): Resolve and separate labels, use the local Jacobian discrepancy estimate, and classify the successive generic echoes. -> Finite exceptional contributions away from generic echoes and finite support for corrected surface brackets.
- **step-junction-difficulty-lower-bound** [claim](#stmt-prop-difficulty-lower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-difficulty-lower): Bound each ceiling-rounding deficit by varying branch excess and compensate with normalization ranks and curve-place weights. -> Nonnegative integer difficulty at every crepant junction.
- **step-normalization-rank-identity** [claim](#stmt-lem-difficulty-ranks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-difficulty-ranks): Prove independence of contracted divisor classes by negativity, then compare cycle-span images on the common open. -> The exact normalization rank difference equals the difference in contracted normalization primes.
- **step-difficulty-monotonicity** [claim](#stmt-prop-difficulty-change) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-difficulty-change): Compare finite exceptional weight sums and cancel the surface-default change with the normalization rank identity. -> Nonincreasing difficulty and strict decrease whenever an exceptional place loses weight.
- **step-positive-surface-detector** [claim](#stmt-lem-positive-surface) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-positive-surface): Use the global generic surface blowup, clear the initial-data denominator at its smooth center, and apply strict discrepancy improvement to ceiling weights. -> A strict exceptional weight loss and strict difficulty drop between adjacent junctions.
- **step-strong-gklt-termination** [claim](#stmt-thm-strong-gklt) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-strong-gklt): First exhaust possible positive-surface difficulty drops, then exclude the remaining tail by strict ambient surface-cycle rank descent. -> Termination for arbitrary qualifying small diagrams on strong generalized klt models.
- **step-elementary-gdlt-termination** [claim](#stmt-thm-gdlt) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-gdlt): Remove finitely many divisorial steps, apply special termination, delete the whole boundary floor and verify both ample signs. -> Termination of elementary generalized dlt programs with varying bases and choices.
- **step-nonempty-gdlt-lift** [claim](#stmt-lem-nonempty-lift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-nonempty-lift): Lift a negative downstairs curve by a multisection, continue the relative generalized dlt program to nefness, and compare with the prescribed ample model. -> A nonempty finite lift ending at a crepant modification of the same type over the positive model.
- **step-main-glc-termination** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-nonempty-lift-2): Start with a crepant generalized dlt modification and concatenate the nonempty finite lifts of all prescribed downstairs diagrams. -> An impossible infinite elementary generalized dlt sequence, proving the main finiteness theorem.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-prescribed-flip-termination**: Every prescribed flip sequence satisfying the main theorem's geometric, singularity, boundary, nef-data, Cartier, and ample-sign hypotheses is finite.
- **invariant-discrepancy-comparison**: Global discrepancies do not decrease across an allowed small diagram. They increase strictly exactly for places whose centers lie in the exceptional loci; exceptionality is preserved between the sides.
- **invariant-junction-nonnegativity**: The integer difficulty is nonnegative at each crepant junction of the truncated terminal chain.
- **invariant-difficulty-descent**: The difficulty never increases under a coefficient change or a small step of the truncated terminal chain. A strict loss of exceptional-place weight forces a strict loss of difficulty.
- **invariant-surface-cycle-loss**: When the target exceptional locus has no surface and the source exceptional locus contains a surface, the rank of analytic surface classes decreases strictly.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-flip-constructor**: Use the termination theorem to construct the next contraction or flip whenever a symbolic procedure requests one. -> Finiteness of an existing sequence does not supply a general construction of its steps or prove that every requested step exists.
- **anti-use-effective-runtime-bound**: Translate qualitative flip termination into an explicit runtime or iteration bound for a computer implementation. -> The proof stabilizes tails, extracts global places, and uses geometric cycle ranks without furnishing a finite input representation or a computational resource bound.
- **anti-use-intermediate-nonnegativity**: Require the difficulty to remain nonnegative on every intermediate model between terminal junctions. -> The construction explicitly allows negative local brackets and negative difficulty on intermediate models. The termination argument uses the lower bound at junctions.
- **anti-use-unconditional-relative-termination**: Treat the finite relative run for gklt data as a theorem that every choice of relative steps terminates. -> The auxiliary proposition supplies existence of a finite run over a fixed base, while its gdlt assertion supplies continuation. These assertions have distinct quantifiers.
- **anti-use-local-place-count**: Replace the global discrepancy-two place count in the branch inequality by an unrestricted count of local divisors near singular points. -> Local pieces can represent the same global divisor. The curve argument retains monodromy information to establish distinct global exceptional components.
- **anti-use-lift-isomorphism**: Assume the endpoint of a lifted downstairs flip is isomorphic to the prescribed positive model. -> The lift produces a projective bimeromorphic crepant gdlt modification, which may retain exceptional primes with coefficient one.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result proves qualitative termination for sequences of compact Kähler fourfolds and global divisorial places, rather than a finite constructive input-output problem. Its conclusion is "Then the sequence is finite." The proof uses crepant extraction, stabilization of tails, Hodge-theoretic branch estimates, and analytic cycle ranks. These existence and finiteness arguments do not provide an executable representation of arbitrary geometric inputs or an independent finite decision oracle. Testing ceiling arithmetic alone would not certify the geometric termination theorem.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The termination theorem concerns prescribed geometric flip sequences. It supplies neither a software procedure for producing the diagrams nor a finite representation on which their hypotheses can be decided. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Loss of analytic cycle classes concerns compact Kähler spaces and proper bimeromorphic transformations. The paper gives no construction connecting these transformations to game states or simulation updates. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The common-model lemma constructs a smooth dominating geometric space through projective modifications. It does not give an executable input representation or a decision procedure for a geometric analysis tool. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Relative continuation provides an elementary negative step under geometric hypotheses, and the gklt assertion provides a finite run over a fixed base. These are mathematical existence assertions rather than a finite algorithm with a specified computational model. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite label set records global divisorial places and transported boundary coefficients on terminal junctions. Its finiteness does not supply a data structure for representing analytic spaces, places, or their centers. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The decomposition lemma concerns derived direct images and perverse middle extensions on analytic supports. It specifies no finite arithmetic workload, memory layout, or parallel construction suitable for an accelerator. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The branch inequality bounds normalization branches by analytic cycle ranks and ordinary discrepancy places. These geometric counts do not define a cryptographic primitive or a security assumption. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The difficulty decreases under boundary coefficient changes and geometric small steps on a truncated terminal chain. The paper supplies no construction identifying those moves with learning updates or the difficulty with a training objective. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The nef-and-ample comparison factors a geometric model through a prescribed ample model using effective exceptional corrections. It supplies no feasible-set encoding, objective function, or optimisation algorithm for operational problems. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Special termination eventually separates exceptional loci from generalized log canonical centers in elementary gdlt sequences. It does not construct a protocol or relate those centers to distributed participants or shared state. |
| other (Birational and complex analytic geometry) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The crepant gdlt modification is a concrete mathematical construction: it replaces rational glc data by a strong compact Kähler gdlt model through a projective bimeromorphic morphism, preserving the adjoint by pullback. Its use is theoretical and requires adaptation before any computational implementation. |

The resolution may simultaneously principalize finitely many specified generically nonzero coherent ideals and resolve finitely many specified divisors.           "anchor": "stmt-prop-terminal-junctions",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that the sequence above is infinite. After passing to a tail, there are a number $\\varepsilon>0$ and a finite set $\\mathcal I$ of global places such that, at every remaining index $i$, $$\\label{eq:stable-low-places}\n \\begin{gathered}\n a_i(E)\\geq\\varepsilon\\quad\\text{for every global place }E,\n \\\\\n \\mathcal I=\n \\{E:E\\text{ is exceptional over }X_i,\\ a_i(E)\\leq 1\\}.\n \\end{gathered}$$ There are projective bimeromorphic crepant morphisms $$\\label{eq:terminal-junction}\n \\begin{gathered}\n h_i:(Y_i,\\Theta_i+\\mathbf{M})\\longrightarrow (X_i,B_i+\\mathbf{M}),\n \\\\\n K_{Y_i}+\\Theta_i+M_{Y_i}=h_i^*D_i,\n \\end{gathered}$$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Each $Y_i$ is a strong normal irreducible compact Kähler fourfold, $\\Theta_i$ is effective, and $(Y_i,\\Theta_i+\\mathbf{M})$ is generalized terminal. The exceptional prime divisors of $h_i$ represent exactly the places in $\\mathcal I$.*\n\n\n2.  *For $E\\in\\mathcal I$, let $S_{i,E}$ be its prime on $Y_i$. Define $$\\label{eq:junction-coefficient-change}\n     \\Theta_i'=\n     \\Theta_i-\\sum_{E\\in\\mathcal I}\n           \\bigl(a_{i+1}(E)-a_i(E)\\bigr)S_{i,E}.$$ Starting with $(Y_i,\\Theta_i'+\\mathbf{M})$, a finite, possibly empty, sequence of small elementary steps over $Z_i$ ends at $(Y_{i+1},\\Theta_{i+1}+\\mathbf{M})$. All its models are strong normal irreducible compact Kähler fourfolds, all its pairs are generalized terminal, and its boundary and nef data are transported as in an elementary program. In particular, every global place is exceptional over one model of this connecting sequence if and only if it is exceptional over all of them.*\n\n3.  *There is one finite label set $\\mathcal H$ for the primes supplied by $\\mathcal I$ and by the components of $B_0$. Write $S_h$ for the current strict transform of a label and $b_h$ for its current coefficient. Labels are retained when their coefficient is zero. On the entire chain of coefficient changes and small steps, $$\\Theta=\\sum_{h\\in\\mathcal H}b_hS_h,\n                        \\qquad 0\\leq b_h<1,$$ and every $b_h$ is nonincreasing. Call a label *varying* if its coefficient sequence is not eventually constant, and *fixed* otherwise. After a further tail is chosen, all fixed coefficients are constant. Every varying label comes from $\\mathcal I$ and has positive coefficient at every remaining stage.",

        }

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-ambient-fourfold-category** (hypothesis): The main termination theorem requires normal irreducible globally Weil Q-factorial compact Kähler fourfolds throughout the prescribed sequence. Blocked use: ['Extend the theorem to higher-dimensional spaces.', 'Apply it to discrete state transitions without a construction satisfying the geometric hypotheses.']
- **limit-rational-effective-boundary** (hypothesis): The boundary must be effective, rational, of finite support and transported by strict transform with the stated coefficient range. Blocked use: ['Substitute an arbitrary real boundary.', 'Allow negative boundary coefficients or introduce new boundary components during the prescribed sequence.']
- **limit-fixed-absolute-nef-carrier** (hypothesis): The nef b-divisor is fixed and represented by analytically nef rational Cartier data on a projective modification. Relative nefness of an unrelated trace is insufficient for the main theorem. Blocked use: ['Change the nef b-divisor between steps.', 'Replace the projective analytically nef carrier by unspecified higher class data.']
- **limit-cartier-compatible-adjoints** (hypothesis): The adjoints must be rational Cartier and canonical representatives must be transported compatibly. The actual-divisor comparisons require these identifications. Blocked use: ['Compare arbitrary independently chosen canonical representatives.', 'Use adjoint pullbacks when the required Cartier property has not been established.']
- **limit-initial-generalized-log-canonicity** (hypothesis): Generalized log canonicity of the initial pair is a required hypothesis, not a consequence of rational boundary coefficients alone. Blocked use: ['Invoke termination for initial data with negative generalized discrepancy.', 'Check boundary coefficients while omitting generalized discrepancies.']
- **limit-projective-small-opposite-signs** (hypothesis): Every prescribed diagram must satisfy projectivity, smallness, connected fibers, nontriviality and the opposite relative ample signs. The main theorem does not directly cover divisorial contractions. Blocked use: ['Replace projective contractions by arbitrary proper morphisms.', 'Drop either relative ample sign.', 'Apply the main small-diagram theorem directly to a sequence containing divisorial contractions.']
- **limit-auxiliary-strong-factoriality** (formal_scope): The relative auxiliary constructions require strong models. This global sheaf condition neither asserts local factoriality nor adds a strong-factoriality assumption to the original main sequence. Blocked use: ['Use relative continuation or exact extraction on a merely globally Weil Q-factorial model without the required auxiliary modification.', 'Assume arbitrary analytic open subsets are Q-factorial.']
- **limit-relative-existence-quantifiers** (formal_scope): The auxiliary generalized klt relative-program assertion supplies a finite run over a fixed bimeromorphic base. It does not itself prove termination of every choice of relative program; generalized dlt continuation is a separate assertion. Blocked use: ['Replace existence of a finite relative run by universal termination.', 'Use generalized dlt continuation alone as a stopping theorem.']
- **limit-pair-dependent-tail-stabilization** (nonconstructive): The discrepancy floor belongs to the fixed pair. Stabilization of low places and coefficient defaults is obtained by passing to tails of a hypothetical infinite sequence, without an effective cutoff supplied here. Blocked use: ['Assume a universal discrepancy floor across unrelated pairs.', 'Turn the tail argument into a computable stopping index from arbitrary finite input.']
- **limit-marked-surface-lattice-denominator** (formal_scope): The marked-surface lattice controls recurring surface centers of extracted places. Its denominator is distinct from the later difficulty denominator, which need not clear all marked-surface discrepancy values. Blocked use: ['Require the difficulty denominator to clear every value in the marked-surface lattice.', 'Infer a bound on the number of transverse exceptional curves from the grouped-label calculation.']
- **limit-branch-terminal-image-global-places** (hypothesis): The branch inequality requires an ordinary terminal source and a reduced divisor union whose image has dimension at most one under a projective bimeromorphic morphism. Its curve-place count identifies global divisors rather than independent local pieces. Blocked use: ['Apply the branch inequality to divisors with surface images.', 'Replace ordinary terminality by an unchecked weaker singularity assumption.', 'Count separate local branches as distinct global discrepancy places.']
- **limit-cycle-ranks-not-chern-classes** (formal_scope): The normalization terms are ranks of spans of analytic surface fundamental classes in rational homology. They are not second Chern classes. Blocked use: ['Replace normalization surface-cycle ranks by characteristic-class values.', 'Use a generic numerical invariant in the branch or difficulty correction.']
- **limit-local-echo-subtraction-before-summation** (formal_scope): Generic echo contributions may recur over infinitely many surfaces. The defaults must be subtracted separately at each surface before the surface sum is formed; removing only the first generic blowup is insufficient. Blocked use: ['Form an uncorrected global sum of all positive exceptional weights.', 'Subtract only the first echo.', 'Subtract aggregate defaults after separately summing potentially infinite raw contributions.']
- **limit-junction-only-lower-bound** (edge_case): Nonnegativity of difficulty is asserted at crepant junctions. Local surface brackets and difficulty on intermediate models may be negative. Blocked use: ['Require a nonnegative difficulty at every connector stage.', 'Use intermediate difficulty as an independently bounded termination measure.']
- **limit-positive-detector-endpoint-lattice** (formal_scope): The positive-surface detector uses the fixed initial-data denominator at the smooth positive endpoint. No denominator assertion for its negative-side discrepancy is required. Blocked use: ['Assume every discrepancy along the sequence belongs to the detector lattice.', 'Infer a ceiling-weight drop from strict discrepancy improvement without the endpoint lattice argument.']
- **limit-floor-deletion-after-special-termination** (hypothesis): The generalized dlt reduction deletes the entire boundary floor only after special termination makes it disjoint from adjacent exceptional loci. Preservation of both ample signs is checked separately. Blocked use: ['Delete an arbitrary part of the floor and assume every zero discrepancy becomes positive.', 'Delete the floor before establishing avoidance of the exceptional loci.', 'Assume the original ample signs survive an arbitrary boundary change.']
- **limit-lift-nonempty-crepant-not-isomorphic** (formal_scope): A prescribed downstairs flip has a nonempty finite generalized dlt lift ending at a projective crepant modification of the positive model. The endpoint need not be isomorphic to that model. Blocked use: ['Identify the lifted endpoint with the positive downstairs model.', 'Allow empty lifts when concatenating lifts to derive the contradiction.', 'Discard coefficient-one exceptional primes from the endpoint data.']
- **limit-no-executable-general-termination-oracle** (nonconstructive): The proof establishes qualitative finiteness of existing geometric sequences. A finite computational input representation, a general flip constructor and an effective universal flip-count bound are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Claim an executable decision oracle for arbitrary analytic flip sequences.', 'Treat tests of ceiling arithmetic as certification of the geometric theorem.', 'Assign runtime or memory complexity to the main termination argument.']

Then the sequence is finite.",

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Every sequence of generalized log canonical flips on a normal irreducible globally Weil ℚ-factorial compact Kähler fourfold terminates, provided the flips are projective small diagrams with the stated opposite ample signs. The boundary is rational, and the nef b-divisor is fixed and represented by an analytically nef ℚ-Cartier divisor on a projective modification. No scaling rule or pseudo-effectivity assumption is required. The theorem concerns existing flip sequences; it does not assert the existence of all contractions or flips.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Termination of flips rules out an endless sequence of small birational improvements in the minimal model program. Since flips contract no divisors, their number cannot be bounded by counting divisors that disappear. In dimension four, exceptional surfaces bring places centered on surfaces and curves into the argument. On compact Kähler spaces, the contractions may be projective even when the ambient fourfold has no ample line bundle.

Shokurov’s difficulty counts exceptional divisors with small discrepancy to measure the improvement of singularities <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[ShokurovNonvanishing](#ref-ShokurovNonvanishing), Definition 2.15 and Corollaries 2.16–2.17]. Kawamata, Matsuda, and Matsuki combined it with the rank of surface cycle classes to prove termination of terminal fourfold flips in the algebraic setting <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[KawamataMatsudaMatsuki1987](#ref-KawamataMatsudaMatsuki1987), Theorem 5-1-15]. For boundaries, Fujino’s corrected argument for canonical fourfold pairs exhibits the recurring contributions from successive blowups over a codimension-two center <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Fujino2005Addendum](#ref-Fujino2005Addendum), Example 2.1 and Theorems 5.1–5.2]. Alexeev, Hacon, and Kawamata developed a weighted difficulty that subtracts these contributions and tracks cycles on normalized boundary components <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[AlexeevHaconKawamata2007](#ref-AlexeevHaconKawamata2007), Section 2]. Discrepancy improvement and the behavior of boundary cycles also guide the proof here.

Generalized pairs place part of the adjoint data on a higher birational model: a nef divisor there contributes its trace on the space carrying the boundary <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[BirkarZhang2016](#ref-BirkarZhang2016), Definitions 1.4 and 4.1]. For algebraic generalized pairs, Hacon and Moraga gave a termination reduction using weak Zariski decompositions <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[HaconMoraga2020](#ref-HaconMoraga2020), Theorem 1]. Termination for pseudo-effective generalized log canonical fourfolds with NQC nef data was proved by Chen and Tsakanikas <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[ChenTsakanikas2023](#ref-ChenTsakanikas2023), Theorem 1.1] and by Moraga <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Moraga2025](#ref-Moraga2025), Theorem 4.4]. Here NQC means a nonnegative real combination of nef $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisors on the higher model. Han, Liu, and Zhuang proved termination of ordinary klt fourfold minimal model programs when the boundary is big over the fixed algebraic base <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[HanLiuZhuang2025](#ref-HanLiuZhuang2025), Corollary 3.2(3)].

Hacon and Xie formulate a termination conjecture for generalized klt pairs with higher $(1,1)$-class data on compact Kähler spaces <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[HaconXie2026](#ref-HaconXie2026), Conjecture 1.15]. The generalized klt case of the theorem below treats a four-dimensional rational-divisor form of that question under the stated projectivity and global Weil $\mathbb{Q}$-factoriality assumptions; the theorem also permits generalized log canonical singularities. Its ambient spaces may be nonprojective, and the prescribed small diagrams may vary their bases and choices under the hypotheses below.

A normal compact complex space $X$ is *globally Weil $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial* if every prime Weil divisor defined on all of $X$ is $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier and some positive reflexive power of its canonical sheaf is invertible. Projective morphisms of complex spaces are understood in the analytic sense: they admit a relatively ample holomorphic line bundle. A $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor is relatively ample if a positive Cartier multiple is relatively ample.

For a normal compact Kähler space $X$, a rational nef b-divisor $\mathbf{M}$ in this paper is represented by a $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor $M'$ on a normal compact Kähler space $X'$ with a projective bimeromorphic map $\pi:X'\to X$. We require $c_1(M')$ to lie in the closure of the Kähler cone in real Bott–Chern cohomology. On a model dominating $X'$, the trace $M_W$ is the pullback of $M'$; on another model it is the pushforward from a common higher model. The divisor $M'$ need not be effective.

<a id="eq:discrepancy-definition"></a>
We use compatible actual canonical divisor representatives on the birational models under consideration. Given an effective rational Weil divisor $B$ of finite support for which $D_X=K_X+B+M_X$ is $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier, choose a smooth proper bimeromorphic model $p:W\to X$ dominating $X'$ and write $$\label{eq:discrepancy-definition}
 K_W+\Delta_W+M_W=p^*(K_X+B+M_X).$$ A *global divisorial place* is a prime divisor on a normal proper bimeromorphic model, with primes identified by strict transform on common higher models. Its log discrepancy is $$a(E;X,B+\mathbf{M})=1-\operatorname{coeff}_E(\Delta_W)$$ on a model carrying $E$. The pair is *generalized log canonical* if this number is nonnegative for every global divisorial place, including the primes on $X$. These places are defined on birational models; the definition does not depend on a field of global meromorphic functions.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $X_0$ be a normal irreducible globally Weil $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial compact Kähler fourfold. Let $B_0$ be an effective rational Weil divisor of finite support with coefficients in $[0,1]$. Let $\mathbf{M}$ be represented by an analytically nef $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor on a projective bimeromorphic normal compact Kähler model of $X_0$. Fix compatible actual canonical divisor representatives on all birational models under consideration. Suppose that $D_0=K_{X_0}+B_0+M_{X_0}$ is $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier and that $(X_0,B_0+\mathbf{M})$ is generalized log canonical.*

*Consider a sequence of normal irreducible globally Weil $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial compact Kähler fourfolds $$X_0\dashrightarrow X_1\dashrightarrow X_2\dashrightarrow\cdots.$$ On $X_i$, let $B_i$ be the strict transform of $B_0$, let $M_i=M_{X_i}$ be the trace of the same b-divisor, and assume that $D_i=K_{X_i}+B_i+M_i$ is $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier. Transport the canonical representatives under the induced identifications of prime divisors. Suppose that every step is given by a diagram <a id="eq:flip-diagram"></a>$$\label{eq:flip-diagram}
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large]
 X_i \arrow[r,"f_i"] & Z_i & X_{i+1}\arrow[l,"f_i^+"']
\end{tikzcd}$$ where $Z_i$ is normal compact Kähler, both maps are projective small bimeromorphic morphisms with connected fibers, neither is an isomorphism, and $$-D_i\text{ is }f_i\text{-ample},\qquad
 D_{i+1}\text{ is }f_i^+\text{-ample}.$$ Here *small* means that the exceptional locus contains no prime divisor. Then the sequence is finite.*

</div>

We call a diagram satisfying the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> a *flip* for the indicated adjoint. The theorem applies to arbitrary choices of such diagrams. Taking $\mathbf{M}=0$ gives the ordinary log canonical case.

The proof uses the relative constructions and special termination for generalized pairs in <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[KahlerAuxiliary](#ref-KahlerAuxiliary)], whose precise forms we recall in Section <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a>.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Proof strategy

We first prove termination for generalized klt pairs on models satisfying the stronger factoriality condition specified in Section <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a>. Discrepancy monotonicity and finiteness for the initial pair make the set of exceptional places of discrepancy at most one constant on a tail. Extracting it gives crepant models $h_i:Y_i\to X_i$ whose remaining exceptional discrepancies are greater than one; we call them *terminal junctions*. Each downstairs flip connects two junctions by a decrease of the extracted boundary coefficients followed by a finite, possibly empty, program of small steps. This construction adapts the projective argument of <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[ProjectiveLCFourfolds](#ref-ProjectiveLCFourfolds), Section 3, Proposition 3.4].

The extracted coefficients may still vary. When an extracted divisor maps onto a surface, a transverse smooth surface cuts its general fiber into exceptional curves. Adjunction and negative definiteness bound the entries of the grouped intersection matrix. The fixed rational boundary and nef data then confine the discrepancies of extracted places with surface centers to a finite set. A downstairs flip strictly raises the discrepancy of a place whose center lies in its exceptional locus. It follows that, on every junction of a tail, each coefficient that still varies belongs to a divisor mapping onto a curve or a point of $X_i$.

The branch bound of Theorem <a href="#thm:branch" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:branch">17</a> applies to a reduced union $Q$ of divisors on a terminal fourfold $Y$ when a projective bimeromorphic morphism $Y\to X$ of normal irreducible compact Kähler fourfolds sends $Q$ to a set of dimension at most one. A surface in $Q$ may have several prime divisors above it in the disjoint normalization of its components. The theorem bounds the total excess of branches by the ranks of surface cycle classes on those normalizations and the number of global ordinary discrepancy-two places centered on singular curves in $Q$. It is formulated independently of any boundary or crepant equation.

The word *global* is essential. Projective topology over the curve image relates the branch excess to local systems along the singular curves. Saito’s projective analytic decomposition theorem and a Hodge-filtration argument on a compact Kähler resolution give the needed purity. A Quot parameter argument gives finite monodromy. On a Stein neighborhood of a compact core carrying all loops of the curve, we apply Fujino’s local small $\mathbb{Q}$-factorialization theorem; fiber degrees and blowups then produce local ordinary discrepancy-two places. A canonical discrepancy comparison shows that they occur as divisors on one global resolution, and the retained loops keep those global divisors distinct. The normalization and parameter methods follow <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[ProjectiveLCFourfolds](#ref-ProjectiveLCFourfolds), Sections 5–6]; the analytic purity argument and the passage to distinct global divisors are given here.

Section <a href="#sec:difficulty" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:difficulty">6</a> constructs the difficulty by counting exceptional places of discrepancy below two with ceiling weights, subtracting generic blowup contributions locally before summation, and adding cycle-rank corrections on normalized boundary components. The branch bound makes it nonnegative at junctions. Across a small step, the cycle-rank change cancels the change in the local subtractions, leaving a sum of nonnegative weight losses. This adapts the locally corrected difficulty in <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[ProjectiveLCFourfolds](#ref-ProjectiveLCFourfolds), Section 4].

A surface in the positive exceptional locus of a downstairs flip forces a drop of at least one in the difficulty between its terminal junctions. Only finitely many flips can do this. Every remaining flip has an exceptional surface only on the negative side, so the rank of analytic surface classes on the ambient $X_i$ decreases. This proves termination for generalized klt pairs on these auxiliary models. Special termination and deletion of the whole boundary floor reduce elementary generalized dlt programs to that case. We then use relative continuation and this gdlt termination to lift each diagram of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> to a finite program whose endpoint maps crepantly to the next downstairs model. A negative curve downstairs has a negative multisection upstairs, so each lift is nonempty. Their concatenation proves Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

<a id="sec:preliminaries"></a>
### Generalized pairs and auxiliary programs

The relative constructions used in the proof take place on models with a stronger global factoriality property than the one in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. We first specify that property and the generalized pair conventions for those constructions. We then record the precise forms of the auxiliary results that will be used.

The auxiliary definitions and statements in this section use reduced, irreducible, second-countable complex analytic spaces as their ambient spaces.

<a id="stmt-def-strong-factoriality"></a>
<div id="def:strong-factoriality" class="definition">

**Definition 2** (Global strong factoriality). A normal compact complex space $T$ is *globally strongly $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial* if, for every coherent rank-one reflexive sheaf $\mathcal F$ on all of $T$, some positive reflexive power $$\mathcal F^{[m]}=(\mathcal F^{\otimes m})^{**},\qquad m>0,$$ is an invertible sheaf.

</div>

This condition implies global Weil $\mathbb{Q}$-factoriality: apply it to the divisorial sheaf of each global prime divisor and to the reflexive canonical sheaf. It is a condition on sheaves on the whole space; it makes no assertion that arbitrary analytic open subsets, or the analytic local rings, are $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial. We use *strong model* to mean a globally strongly $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial model.

A *rational line bundle* on $T$ is an element of $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(T)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}$. An equality of rational line bundles means an isomorphism of holomorphic line bundles after taking a positive common integral multiple. A rank-one reflexive sheaf is *rationally invertible* if a positive reflexive power is invertible. On a projective morphism $T\to V$, a rational line bundle is *relatively nef* if its degree is nonnegative on every compact irreducible curve in a fiber, and is *relatively ample* if a positive integral multiple is relatively ample. For the absolute nef data in this paper, nefness means that the first Chern class lies in the closure of the Kähler cone, as in the introduction; it implies relative nefness for every morphism under consideration.

The auxiliary constructions are stated for rational generalized *b-line data*. On a normal compact space $T$, these consist of an effective rational Weil divisor $B$ of finite support, a projective modification $p:W\to T$, and a rational line bundle $M_W$ on $W$ that is nef absolutely, or nef over the specified base of a relative argument. It is pulled back on every higher model; we continue to denote the resulting b-object by $\mathbf{M}$. After clearing a denominator, proper direct image followed by double dual defines its reflexive trace $M_T$. The adjoint $D_T=K_T+B+M_T$ is required to be rationally invertible. Here $K_T$ in the b-line formulation is the reflexive canonical sheaf. On a smooth log resolution projective over $T$ and carrying the data, the natural meromorphic comparison over the isomorphism locus defines the crepant boundary by $$K_W+B_W+M_W=p^*D_T.$$ The log discrepancy of a global place $E$ is $a(E;T,B+\mathbf{M})=1-\operatorname{coeff}_E(B_W)$ on a model carrying it. All statements about places below concern the global places defined in the introduction. A global place is exceptional over $T$ if its center on $T$ has codimension at least two.

We abbreviate *generalized klt*, *generalized lc*, and *generalized dlt* to gklt, glc, and gdlt. A pair is gklt if all of its global log discrepancies are positive, and glc if they are all nonnegative. It is *generalized terminal* if it is gklt and $a(E;T,B+\mathbf{M})>1$ for every place exceptional over $T$. A *generalized log canonical center* is the center of a global place of log discrepancy zero. A glc pair is gdlt if there is a Zariski-open set $T^\circ\subset T$ on which $(T^\circ,B|_{T^\circ})$ is simple normal crossing and the b-data descend, and which contains the general point of every generalized log canonical center. The carrier and subsequent log resolutions may be chosen isomorphic on this open. The zero-discrepancy centers there are strata of the coefficient-one boundary. These are the conventions of <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[KahlerAuxiliary](#ref-KahlerAuxiliary), Convention 9.1 and Definition 9.2].

We explain the relation between this formulation and the actual divisors in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. A $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor determines a rational line bundle. If $mM_W$ is integral Cartier, then on the normal target the reflexive sheaf $$\bigl(p_*\mathcal O_W(mM_W)\bigr)^{**}$$ agrees with the divisorial sheaf $\mathcal O_T(mp_*M_W)$: the two agree at every codimension-one point, where the modification is an isomorphism, and normality gives uniqueness of the reflexive extension. The same comparison on a common higher model is compatible with pullback and with transport across a small map. The canonical trace is the canonical sheaf of the target. These facts are <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[KahlerAuxiliary](#ref-KahlerAuxiliary), Lemma 9.3(i)–(ii)]. The natural meromorphic comparisons therefore agree with the comparisons using the compatible actual canonical representatives in <a href="#eq:discrepancy-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:discrepancy-definition">[eq:discrepancy-definition]</a>. In every application below the b-line discrepancies are exactly the discrepancies of that equation, and a crepant b-line identity is the corresponding equality of actual rational divisors. A common projective carrier for any finite part of our birational constructions carries the pullback of the original analytically nef divisor, which is relatively nef over each base used in that part. A single carrier dominating an infinite sequence is not required.

Here is the elementary step used in the auxiliary programs. Suppose that the source $T$ and the output $T_1$ are strong normal compact Kähler spaces, and write $D_T$ and $D_{T_1}$ for their rationally invertible adjoints. An *elementary negative step* is either a projective bimeromorphic divisorial contraction $f:T\to T_1$ for which $-D_T$ is $f$-ample, or a diagram $$T\xrightarrow{\ f\ }Z\xleftarrow{\ f^+\ }T_1$$ of projective small bimeromorphic morphisms to a normal compact Kähler space with $-D_T$ $f$-ample and $D_{T_1}$ $f^+$-ample. The degree functionals of the curves contracted on the negative side span one nonzero ray when paired with global rational line bundles on $T$. In the small case the positive side is the relative Proj of the actual adjoint algebra: after choosing a positive multiple of $D_T$ represented by a holomorphic line bundle $\mathcal L$, it is $$T_1=\mathop{\mathrm{Proj}}_Z\bigoplus_{m\geq0} f_*\mathcal L^{\otimes m}.$$ The boundary is pushed forward in the divisorial case and strictly transformed in the small case, and the same b-data are used on common higher models. With the compatible actual canonical representatives, a divisorial step satisfies $D_{T_1}=f_*D_T$. If the step is *over $V$*, all its maps commute with the given maps to $V$. In the constructions used below the contraction maps have connected fibers, and a small step has nonisomorphic morphisms on both sides; the latter properties are also verified in Section <a href="#sec:comparisons" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:comparisons">3</a>. This is the form of <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[KahlerAuxiliary](#ref-KahlerAuxiliary), Definition 9.6]. Its one-ray condition concerns degrees of global line bundles and imposes no condition on the relative Bott–Chern dimension. A diagram in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> need not be elementary.

<a id="stmt-prop-low-places"></a>
<div id="prop:low-places" class="proposition">

**Proposition 3** (Low places and projective realization). *Let $(T,B+\mathbf{M})$ be gklt rational generalized data on a normal compact Kähler space, with effective boundary of finite support and an absolutely nef rational datum carried by a projective modification. Fix a projective log resolution carrying the datum. There is $\varepsilon>0$ such that every global place satisfies $$a(E;T,B+\mathbf{M})\geq\varepsilon,$$ and only finitely many places exceptional over $T$ satisfy $$a(E;T,B+\mathbf{M})<1+\varepsilon.$$ This finite set can be represented simultaneously by prime divisors on a smooth model projective over $T$. In particular the exceptional places of discrepancy at most one form a finite set with such a realization.*

</div>

This is the rational nef specialization of <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[KahlerAuxiliary](#ref-KahlerAuxiliary), Lemma 4.2(i)]. The positive number $\varepsilon$ belongs to this one pair; uniformity along a sequence will follow from discrepancy monotonicity.

<a id="stmt-prop-extraction"></a>
<div id="prop:extraction" class="proposition">

**Proposition 4** (Exact extraction). *Let $(T,B+\mathbf{M})$ be rational gklt data on a strong normal compact Kähler space, with an absolutely nef rational line bundle on a projective carrier and rationally invertible adjoint. For any specified finite set $\mathcal E$ of places exceptional over $T$ with $a(E;T,B+\mathbf{M})\leq1$, there is a projective bimeromorphic morphism $h:U\to T$ such that $U$ is a strong normal compact Kähler space, the $h$-exceptional prime divisors are exactly the members of $\mathcal E$, and the crepant generalized pair on $U$ is gklt with effective boundary. Thus, writing this boundary as $B_U$, $$K_U+B_U+M_U=h^*(K_T+B+M_T).$$*

</div>

This is <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[KahlerAuxiliary](#ref-KahlerAuxiliary), Proposition 9.11]. Terminality is not part of the extraction assertion. It will follow when $\mathcal E$ is chosen to contain every exceptional place of discrepancy at most one.

<a id="stmt-prop-relative-program"></a>
<div id="prop:relative-program" class="proposition">

**Proposition 5** (Relative programs). *Let $\pi:T\to V$ be a projective bimeromorphic morphism of normal compact Kähler spaces. Suppose that $T$ is strong and that $(T,B+\mathbf{M})$ is rational gdlt, with effective boundary, a projective carrier on which the rational line datum is nef over $V$, and a rationally invertible adjoint $D_T$.*

1.  *If $D_T$ is not relatively nef over $V$, there is an elementary negative step over $V$. Its output is projective over $V$, normal compact Kähler and strong, and is gdlt with the transported boundary and b-data. The same construction is available after every finite prefix whose last adjoint is not relatively nef over $V$.*

2.  *If the initial pair is gklt, there exists a finite, possibly empty, sequence of elementary negative steps over this one fixed base $V$ whose last adjoint is relatively nef over $V$. Its models and transported data have the properties in the first assertion.*

</div>

The two assertions are respectively <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[KahlerAuxiliary](#ref-KahlerAuxiliary), Propositions 9.7 and 9.9]. The first is a continuation assertion for gdlt data. The second asserts the existence of one finite run for gklt data; it does not assert termination of every choice of a relative program.

<a id="stmt-prop-gdlt-modification"></a>
<div id="prop:gdlt-modification" class="proposition">

**Proposition 6** (Crepant gdlt modification). *Let $(T,B+\mathbf{M})$ be rational glc data of dimension at most four on a normal compact Kähler space. Assume that the higher absolutely nef rational line datum is carried by a projective modification and that $K_T+B+M_T$ is rationally invertible. There is a projective bimeromorphic morphism $h:Y\to T$ such that $Y$ is a strong normal compact Kähler space, $(Y,B_Y+\mathbf{M})$ is gdlt with $B_Y\geq0$, and $$K_Y+B_Y+M_Y=h^*(K_T+B+M_T).$$ Every $h$-exceptional prime has coefficient one in $B_Y$, equivalently has generalized log discrepancy zero over $(T,B+\mathbf{M})$.*

</div>

This is <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[KahlerAuxiliary](#ref-KahlerAuxiliary), Proposition 11.4]. The base $T$ need not be strong, and the assertion does not require that every zero-discrepancy place be extracted.

<a id="stmt-thm-special-termination"></a>
<div id="thm:special-termination" class="theorem">

**Theorem 7** (Special termination). *Let $d\leq4$, and let $$(T_0,B_0+\mathbf{M})\dashrightarrow(T_1,B_1+\mathbf{M})
 \dashrightarrow(T_2,B_2+\mathbf{M})\dashrightarrow\cdots$$ be a sequence of elementary steps for rational gdlt data on strong normal compact Kähler spaces of dimension $d$. The boundaries are transported as in an elementary program, and the higher absolutely nef b-line datum is fixed and is carried by projective modifications. There is an index $i_0$ such that, for every $i\geq i_0$, the exceptional loci on both sides of the step are disjoint from every generalized log canonical center on their respective sides. The contraction bases in this sequence may vary.*

</div>

This is <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[KahlerAuxiliary](#ref-KahlerAuxiliary), Theorem 11.3]. We use it for the elementary sequences on strong models. Full termination for four-dimensional gdlt data will be deduced in Section <a href="#sec:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:completion">7</a>.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### Scope of the auxiliary inputs.

The results recalled above use the projective relative arguments in <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[KahlerAuxiliary](#ref-KahlerAuxiliary), Sections 9–11], together with its low-place calculation. Relative continuation is Proposition 9.7 there; the finite run over a fixed bimeromorphic base in Proposition 9.9 uses the local finite-generation argument of Lemma 9.8, and exact extraction in Proposition 9.11 uses that finite run. The special-termination and gdlt modification arguments in Section 11 form an induction on dimension, using the threefold termination theorem of Section 10. These proofs do not invoke the canonical bundle formula or the absolute supporting-contraction induction of Section 6. Indeed, Section 6 uses the threefold termination theorem as an input to its good-model construction; the dependency is in that direction. Thus the use of <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[KahlerAuxiliary](#ref-KahlerAuxiliary)] here does not require its absolute contraction or minimal-model theorems.

The imports from <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[CanonicalKahler](#ref-CanonicalKahler)] are likewise restricted to its common-model construction (Proposition 2.1), analytic negativity (Lemma 2.2), and terminal smoothness in codimension two (Lemma 2.3). Its fourfold termination theorem is not an input. All relative constructions above retain their stated strong-factoriality hypotheses; these are not additional hypotheses on the given sequence in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

<a id="sec:comparisons"></a>
### Birational comparisons

We compare the adjoints on the two sides of a small diagram and at the ends of a finite relative program. The comparisons locate exactly which global places acquire larger discrepancies. They also give a projective morphism from a nef end to a prescribed ample model, with the nef adjoint equal to the pullback of the ample adjoint. We then record the homological ranks that count contracted divisors and surfaces. All divisor equalities in this section are equalities of actual rational divisors, with the compatible canonical representatives and the fixed b-divisor $\mathbf{M}$ of Section <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a>.

For a projective morphism, a rational Cartier divisor is called *relatively nef* here if it has nonnegative degree on every compact irreducible curve contracted by the morphism. We use the analytic negativity lemma in the following precise form <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[CanonicalKahler](#ref-CanonicalKahler), Lemma 2.2]: if $r:V\to T$ is a projective bimeromorphic morphism of normal complex spaces, $H$ is a $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor on $V$, $-H$ is relatively nef, and $r_*H\geq0$, then $H\geq0$. Neither compactness nor a singularity hypothesis is part of this input. A divisor is *exceptional over $T$* if every prime in its support has image of codimension at least two in $T$.

We will also use two consequences of normality. An effective $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor pulls back effectively under a dominant morphism: after clearing its index, a local meromorphic equation with no divisorial poles is holomorphic on a normal space. Such a divisor has nonnegative degree on a compact curve not contained in its support, by restricting its effective section to the normalization of the curve.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Common models and the nef part

The projectivity in the hypotheses permits all comparisons in a finite part of the argument to be made on one compact Kähler resolution. We give the construction for the diagrams used here; compare the common-model construction of <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[CanonicalKahler](#ref-CanonicalKahler), Proposition 2.1].

<a id="stmt-lem-common-projective-models"></a>
<div id="lem:common-projective-models" class="lemma">

**Lemma 8** (Common models with projective maps). *Let a finite collection of normal irreducible compact Kähler spaces be joined, with compatible bimeromorphic identifications, by finitely many projective bimeromorphic morphisms and their inverses. Suppose that a fixed nef b-divisor is carried by a projective bimeromorphic morphism $X'\to X$ to one of these spaces, with analytically nef $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier trace $M'$ on $X'$. Then there is a smooth compact Kähler space $W$ with compatible projective bimeromorphic morphisms to every space in the collection and to $X'$. The resolution may simultaneously principalize finitely many specified generically nonzero coherent ideals and resolve finitely many specified divisors. Its trace $M_W$ is the pullback of $M'$ and is analytically nef.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-common-projective-models"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-common-projective-models</summary>

*Proof.* For a diagram $T\to Z\leftarrow T^+$, take the reduced component of $T\times_ZT^+$ containing the graph over the common isomorphism open. Its projections are projective, and finite normalization preserves projectivity. Adjoin the remaining spaces and the carrier one at a time by the same construction. Resolving the resulting dominating component gives a common smooth model. Analytic resolution and principalization allow the stated simultaneous choices by projective modifications; see <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[BierstoneMilman1997](#ref-BierstoneMilman1997), Theorems 1.6, 1.10 and 13.2(1)]. Base change, restriction to a closed analytic subspace, finite normalization, and composition preserve projectivity. For composition in the present compact setting, one can choose a uniform sufficiently large twist of a relative ample bundle by the pullback of the next one. This also proves compatibility with any finite chain of the small diagrams and divisorial contractions considered below.

A smooth space projective over a compact Kähler space is compact Kähler: a relative ample bundle supplies positivity in the fiber directions, and adding a sufficiently large multiple of a pulled-back Kähler form supplies positivity in all directions. Finally, choose Kähler classes on $X'$ converging to $c_1(M')$. Their pullbacks to $W$ have semipositive representatives. Adding positive multiples tending to zero of a fixed Kähler class on $W$ gives Kähler classes converging to $c_1(M_W)$. Thus $M_W$ is analytically nef. ◻

</details>

In particular, $M_W$ has nonnegative degree on every compact curve. For an arbitrary global place, a further common proper model with a model carrying that place suffices to compare coefficients. This last model need not be projective: projectivity is used to establish an effective divisor on a common model, after which its pullbacks remain effective on every higher proper model.

<a id="stmt-lem-nef-defect"></a>
<div id="lem:nef-defect" class="lemma">

**Lemma 9** (Effective nef defect). *Let $T$ be a normal compact Kähler model, and let $r:W\to T$ be a projective bimeromorphic morphism from a normal compact Kähler space $W$ dominating the fixed projective carrier of $\mathbf{M}$. Assume that $M_T$ is $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier. Then <a id="eq:nef-defect"></a>$$\label{eq:nef-defect}
 J_{W/T}:=r^*M_T-M_W\geq0$$ is an exceptional $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor over $T$. If $s:W'\to W$ is a higher normal model, then $J_{W'/T}=s^*J_{W/T}$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-nef-defect"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-nef-defect</summary>

*Proof.* The trace definition gives $r_*J_{W/T}=0$. On an $r$-contracted curve, $-J_{W/T}=M_W-r^*M_T$ has the same degree as $M_W$, hence has nonnegative degree by projection formula from the nef carrier. Analytic negativity gives $J_{W/T}\geq0$. The final identity follows from functoriality of pullback and the fact that the b-divisor descends on $W$. ◻

</details>

The next lemma explains why hypotheses stated for global generalized places give the local ordinary singularity conditions needed later. It is here that effectivity of the boundary and of the nef defect enters the singularity comparison. The global Weil and strong $\mathbb{Q}$-factoriality conventions ensure the Cartier hypotheses below for the respective models in the proof, since the divisor traces have finite support.

<a id="stmt-lem-ordinary-singularities"></a>
<div id="lem:ordinary-singularities" class="lemma">

**Lemma 10** (From global generalized discrepancies to local singularities). *Let $T$ be a normal compact Kähler space, let $B$ be an effective rational divisor of finite support, and let $\mathbf{M}$ have analytically nef rational data on a projective carrier. Assume that $K_T$, $B$, and $M_T$ are $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier. If every global generalized log discrepancy of $(T,B+\mathbf{M})$ is nonnegative, respectively positive, then the same condition holds for local analytic places. In the positive case, $T$ has ordinary klt singularities locally. If the global generalized discrepancies are positive and every global place exceptional over $T$ has generalized log discrepancy strictly greater than one, then $T$ has ordinary terminal singularities locally. In dimension four the latter conclusion implies $\dim\mathop{\mathrm{Sing}}T\leq1$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-ordinary-singularities"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-ordinary-singularities</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a smooth log resolution $r:W\to T$, projective over $T$ and carrying $\mathbf{M}$, with divisorial exceptional locus and simple normal crossing support for all divisors in the crepant equation $$K_W+\Delta_W+M_W=r^*(K_T+B+M_T).$$ Such a simultaneous resolution is available by Lemma <a href="#lem:common-projective-models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:common-projective-models">8</a>. The assumed inequalities give coefficients at most one, respectively strictly below one, for every global prime of $\Delta_W$. The same bounds hold on their local branches. On any further local model the nef part is pulled back from $W$, so its generalized discrepancies are the ordinary discrepancies of the simple normal crossing subpair $(W,\Delta_W)$. The simple normal crossing discrepancy criterion proves the asserted local generalized inequalities, including when some coefficients of $\Delta_W$ are negative.

Put $J=J_{W/T}$. The ordinary crepant boundary for $(T,0)$ is <a id="eq:ordinary-crepant-boundary"></a>$$\label{eq:ordinary-crepant-boundary}
 \Delta_W^{(0)}=\Delta_W-r^*B-J,
 \qquad K_W+\Delta_W^{(0)}=r^*K_T.$$ Both $r^*B$ and $J$ are effective. After resolving their joint support as above, every coefficient of $\Delta_W^{(0)}$ is therefore strictly below one in the positive case. The same local simple normal crossing criterion gives ordinary klt singularities.

For the terminal assertion, the relative canonical divisor is exceptional, and at an exceptional prime $R$ of $W$ its coefficient is $$\operatorname{coeff}_R(K_W-r^*K_T)
 =a(R;T,B+\mathbf{M})-1+\operatorname{coeff}_R(r^*B+J)>0.$$ These positive coefficients persist on every local branch. A local place exceptional over the smooth space $W$ has ordinary log discrepancy at least two over $W$: the Jacobian determinant of a smooth model carrying it has positive integral order along an exceptional divisor. Its discrepancy over $T$ adds the nonnegative order of $K_W-r^*K_T$, and hence remains greater than one. Local exceptional places already represented by divisors on $W$ satisfy the displayed strict inequality. Thus $T$ is locally ordinary terminal. For a normal complex fourfold with a rational canonical line bundle, local ordinary terminality implies smoothness in codimension two by <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[CanonicalKahler](#ref-CanonicalKahler), Lemma 2.3], giving the last assertion. ◻

</details>

For later calculations, the same equations give, for every global place $E$ followed on a common higher model, <a id="eq:generalized-ordinary-discrepancy"></a>$$\label{eq:generalized-ordinary-discrepancy}
 a(E;T,B+\mathbf{M})
 =a(E;T,0)-\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E(r^*B)-\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E(J_{W/T}).$$ The orders on the right mean coefficients after pullback to that model. They are nonnegative under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:ordinary-singularities" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:ordinary-singularities">10</a>.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Small diagrams and finite programs

We now locate the entire region in which a small step changes discrepancies. All exceptional loci and their images in this subsection are given their reduced structures. We use the graph method of <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[ProjectiveLCFourfolds](#ref-ProjectiveLCFourfolds), Lemma 2.1] and prove the needed analytic comparison, with the fixed b-divisor, below.

<a id="stmt-lem-small-comparison"></a>
<div id="lem:small-comparison" class="lemma">

**Lemma 11** (Comparison for an arbitrary small diagram). *Let $$T\xrightarrow{\ f\ }Z\xleftarrow{\ f^+\ }T^+$$ be a diagram of normal irreducible compact Kähler fourfolds in which $f$ and $f^+$ are projective small bimeromorphic morphisms with connected fibers, and both are nonisomorphisms. Let $(T,B_T+\mathbf{M})$ and $(T^+,B_{T^+}+\mathbf{M})$ have effective rational boundaries related by strict transform and the same fixed rational b-divisor. Transport the canonical representatives by the identification of prime divisors, and assume that the actual adjoints $D_T$ and $D_{T^+}$ are $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier, with $-D_T$ $f$-ample and $D_{T^+}$ $f^+$-ample.*

<a id="eq:small-loci"></a>
<a id="eq:small-dimensions"></a>
*The exceptional images of $f$ and $f^+$ are equal. If their common image is $A$, then $$\label{eq:small-loci}
 L:=\mathop{\mathrm{Exc}}(f)=f^{-1}(A),\qquad
 L^+:=\mathop{\mathrm{Exc}}(f^+)=(f^+)^{-1}(A),$$ the complements are isomorphic, and $$\label{eq:small-dimensions}
 \dim L,\dim L^+\leq2,\qquad \dim A\leq1,
 \qquad \dim L+\dim L^+\geq3.$$*

<a id="eq:small-support"></a>
*Let $G$ be the normalization of the main graph, with projections $p:G\to T$, $q:G\to T^+$ and $h=f\circ p=f^+\circ q$. Then the actual $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor <a id="eq:small-divisor"></a>$$\label{eq:small-divisor}
 F:=p^*D_T-q^*D_{T^+}$$ is effective and exceptional over both $T$ and $T^+$, and $$\label{eq:small-support}
 \mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}F=h^{-1}(A).$$ Moreover, $F\cdot C<0$ for every irreducible curve $C$ contracted by $h$.*

<a id="eq:small-discrepancies"></a>
*For every global place $E$ over these spaces, let $\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E(F)$ denote the coefficient of the pullback of $F$ on any higher proper model dominating $G$ and carrying $E$. Then $$\label{eq:small-discrepancies}
 a(E;T^+,B_{T^+}+\mathbf{M})-a(E;T,B_T+\mathbf{M})=\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E(F)\geq0.$$ The inequality is strict exactly when $\mathop{\mathrm{cent}}_T(E)\subset L$, and this condition is equivalent to $\mathop{\mathrm{cent}}_{T^+}(E)\subset L^+$. A place is exceptional over $T$ if and only if it is exceptional over $T^+$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-small-comparison"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-small-comparison</summary>

*Proof.* We first recall the fiber description for a proper bimeromorphic morphism $v:V\to S$ of normal spaces. Its positive-dimensional-fiber locus is closed analytic. Over its complement the map is finite locally on $S$, hence an isomorphism by normality. Stein factorization shows that every fiber is connected, because its finite bimeromorphic factor over the normal space $S$ is an isomorphism. A reduced compact connected positive-dimensional fiber has no zero-dimensional irreducible component: such a component would be a singleton disjoint from the finitely many other closed components. Hence no point in such a fiber is a local isomorphism point. The exceptional locus of $v$ is exactly the full inverse image of its positive-dimensional-fiber locus.

Let $U\subset Z$ be the open set over which $f$ is an isomorphism. On $U$, $D_T$ defines a $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor $D_U$. The actual Weil coefficients of the two adjoints agree under the identification of prime divisors. Since $f^+$ is small, it follows that $D_{T^+}|_{(f^+)^{-1}U}=(f^+)^*D_U$. A positive-dimensional projective fiber over $U$ would contain a curve on which this divisor has degree zero, contrary to $f^+$-ampleness. Thus $f^+$ is an isomorphism over $U$. Interchanging the two sides and using $f$-ampleness of $-D_T$ gives the reverse inclusion of the isomorphism opens. This proves the equality of exceptional images and <a href="#eq:small-loci" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:small-loci">[eq:small-loci]</a>. Smallness gives $\dim L,\dim L^+\leq2$. Over a general point of each component of $A$ the fiber has dimension at least one, so the fiber-dimension theorem gives $\dim A\leq1$.

The graph projections are projective and surjective, and the map from $G$ to its component in $T\times_ZT^+$ is finite. A prime divisor of either side maps to a prime divisor of $Z$ by smallness. On the graph it therefore corresponds to the same prime place on the other side, with the same adjoint coefficient. Consequently $p_*F=q_*F=0$. On a $p$-contracted curve, $-F$ has the degree of $q^*D_{T^+}$, which is nonnegative. Analytic negativity gives $F\geq0$.

For a curve $C$ contracted by $h$, the projection formula and the two ample signs give $$-F\cdot C=(-D_T)\cdot p_*C+D_{T^+}\cdot q_*C>0.$$ Here an image that is a point contributes zero. At least one image is a curve, because the finite map to the fiber product cannot contract $C$ to a point. Thus at least one summand is strictly positive.

The support of $F$ is contained in $h^{-1}(A)$, since both projections identify the common isomorphism open and the adjoints agree there. Conversely, take $x\in h^{-1}(A)$ and write $z=h(x)$. The map $h:G\to Z$ is proper bimeromorphic, so its fiber over $z$ is connected. It is projective and positive-dimensional: its projection to the positive-dimensional fiber $f^{-1}(z)$ is surjective. The fiber description above places $x$ on a positive-dimensional irreducible component. Successive projective hyperplane cuts through $x$ give an irreducible curve $C$ in that fiber through $x$. If $x$ were outside $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}F$, then $C$ would not be contained in the effective $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor $F$, so $F\cdot C\geq0$. The preceding strict inequality excludes this. Hence <a href="#eq:small-support" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:small-support">[eq:small-support]</a> holds.

Follow a global place $E$ on a common proper model dominating $G$ and the nef carrier. Subtracting its two crepant equations cancels the same trace of $\mathbf{M}$ and gives <a href="#eq:small-discrepancies" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:small-discrepancies">[eq:small-discrepancies]</a>. The order of an effective $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor is positive precisely when the center of the place is contained in its support. Proper images of centers give $$\mathop{\mathrm{cent}}_Z(E)=f(\mathop{\mathrm{cent}}_T(E))=f^+(\mathop{\mathrm{cent}}_{T^+}(E))=h(\mathop{\mathrm{cent}}_G(E)).$$ Together with <a href="#eq:small-loci" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:small-loci">[eq:small-loci]</a> and <a href="#eq:small-support" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:small-support">[eq:small-support]</a>, this proves both center equivalence and the stated exact strictness criterion. If the centers are not contained in these loci, their general points correspond on the common open and the order is zero. Finally, a place centered at a divisor on a normal model is that divisorial place. Smallness identifies these prime divisors on the two sides, proving equivalence of exceptionality.

Since both morphisms are nonisomorphisms, $A$ is nonempty, so $F$ has a prime divisor $R$ in its support. The graph is a fourfold, hence $\dim R=3$. Its finite map to the graph component has image in $L\times_ZL^+$, whence $$3\leq\dim(L\times_ZL^+)\leq\dim L+\dim L^+.$$ This is the remaining inequality in <a href="#eq:small-dimensions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:small-dimensions">[eq:small-dimensions]</a>. ◻

</details>

The discrepancy statement preserves generalized lc and generalized klt inequalities along a small step. It also preserves generalized terminality, because the same places are exceptional on both sides. The isomorphism-open argument did not use nontriviality. In the normal irreducible compact Kähler fourfold setting of Lemma <a href="#lem:small-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:small-comparison">11</a>, if an elementary small step is initially specified by a nonisomorphic negative contraction, a small positive morphism, and the stated Cartier and ample hypotheses, that argument forces the positive morphism to be nonisomorphic as well. Its fibers are connected by normality and Stein factorization, so Lemma <a href="#lem:small-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:small-comparison">11</a> applies. For a finite program we need, in addition, to retain the location of the accumulated correction at its end.

<a id="stmt-lem-program-comparison"></a>
<div id="lem:program-comparison" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:program-comparison"></a>
**Lemma 12** (Comparison along a finite negative program). *Let $Y_0\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow Y_m$, with $m\geq0$, be a finite sequence of normal irreducible compact Kähler fourfolds with $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier adjoints $D_j=K_{Y_j}+B_j+M_{Y_j}$. Suppose the effective rational boundaries are transported by pushforward, the canonical representatives are compatible, and the same b-divisor with a projective carrier is used throughout. Assume each step is either a small diagram satisfying Lemma <a href="#lem:small-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:small-comparison">11</a>, or a projective bimeromorphic divisorial contraction $v_j:Y_j\to Y_{j+1}$ such that $D_{j+1}=(v_j)_*D_j$ and $-D_j$ is $v_j$-ample. These steps extract no prime divisors. On a smooth common model $W$ with projective maps to every $Y_j$ and to the carrier, write $r_j:W\to Y_j$. Then $$\label{eq:program-comparison}
 r_0^*D_0-r_m^*D_m=E,\qquad E\geq0,\qquad (r_m)_*E=0.$$ Every global discrepancy is nondecreasing along the run. If a prime divisor is contracted in a divisorial step, its discrepancy increases strictly at that step, and its discrepancy at the end is strictly larger than before that step.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-program-comparison"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-program-comparison</summary>

*Proof.* For a divisorial step put $E_j=D_j-v_j^*D_{j+1}$. It is exceptional over $Y_{j+1}$ and has strictly negative degree on every $v_j$-contracted curve. Negativity gives $E_j\geq0$. Every point of an exceptional fiber lies on a curve in that fiber, by the connected projective fiber argument in the proof of Lemma <a href="#lem:small-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:small-comparison">11</a>. The negative degree therefore forces that point into $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}E_j$. In particular the coefficient of $E_j$ at each contracted prime is positive. Subtracting crepant equations gives nondecreasing discrepancies and this strict increase. For a small step the same assertions, with an effective correction exceptional over its output, follow from Lemma <a href="#lem:small-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:small-comparison">11</a>.

Pull the corrections of all steps to $W$ and add them. This gives the effective divisor in <a href="#eq:program-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:program-comparison">[eq:program-comparison]</a>. Each prime in a summand is exceptional over that step’s output, and remains exceptional over every later output. Indeed, if that place became a prime divisor on a later normal model, that prime would have a strict-transform prime on each earlier model, since none of the intervening steps extracts divisors. This contradicts its earlier exceptionality. Thus the sum is exceptional over $Y_m$. Its nonnegative coefficients also show that a strict increase from a contracted prime cannot be canceled later. For $m=0$ the correction is zero and the assertions are immediate. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### A nef end and a prescribed ample model

An effective correction exceptional over the end is the form needed to compare a finite program with a prescribed positive side of a diagram. The next argument uses the effective-pushforward form of negativity, since the two corrections need not have the same exceptional primes; compare <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[ProjectiveLCFourfolds](#ref-ProjectiveLCFourfolds), Lemma 2.5] in the projective setting.

<a id="stmt-lem-nef-ample"></a>
<div id="lem:nef-ample" class="lemma">

**Lemma 13** (Nef and ample models). *Let $Y_0$, $Y$, and $T_+$ be normal irreducible compact Kähler spaces with projective bimeromorphic morphisms to a normal compact Kähler space $S$. On a smooth common model $W$ over $S$, projective over these spaces, let $P_0$, $P$, and $P_+$ be the pullbacks of respective $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisors $D_0$, $D_Y$, and $D_+$. Suppose that $$P_0=P+E_Y=P_++E_+,$$ where $E_Y\geq0$ is exceptional over $Y$ and $E_+\geq0$ is exceptional over $T_+$. If $D_Y$ is nef over $S$ and $D_+$ is ample over $S$, then $P=P_+$ as actual divisors. The induced bimeromorphic map is a projective morphism $\varphi:Y\to T_+$ over $S$, and $D_Y=\varphi^*D_+$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-nef-ample"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-nef-ample</summary>

*Proof.* Write $r:W\to Y$ and $t:W\to T_+$, and put $H=P-P_+=E_+-E_Y$. Then $r_*H=r_*E_+\geq0$. On an $r$-contracted curve, $-H$ has the degree of $P_+$, which is nonnegative because the curve lies over a point of $S$. Negativity gives $H\geq0$. Likewise $t_*(-H)=t_*E_Y\geq0$, while $H$ is $t$-nef because $P$ comes from an $S$-nef divisor. Negativity applied to $-H$ gives $H\leq0$. Thus $P=P_+$.

For a curve $C$ in an $r$-fiber, equality gives $t^*D_+\cdot C=P\cdot C=0$. The image $t(C)$ lies in an $S$-fiber, where $D_+$ is ample, so $t(C)$ is a point. Every $r$-fiber is projective and connected. If an irreducible component of one such fiber had positive-dimensional image under $t$, a curve in that projective image and projective hyperplane cuts of its inverse image would supply a curve on which $t$ is nonconstant. Thus each component maps to a point, and connectedness makes those points equal. The map $t$ is constant on every $r$-fiber.

The reduced image of $(r,t):W\to Y\times_ST_+$ is a proper analytic graph with one-point fibers over $Y$. Its projection to $Y$ is therefore finite and bimeromorphic, hence an isomorphism by normality of $Y$. This gives the morphism $\varphi$.
